# CLAUDE.md — Hornelore Agent Env Notes

**Read this first at the start of every session.** These are persistent operational facts, not a task log.

## Universal pivot framing (2026-06-14)

**Hornelore is Lorevox.** The Horne family is *tenant zero* — the first real user, whose sessions hardened the system — not a special case in the architecture. Every WO, prompt, and acceptance gate from this date forward is written against the universal assumption: Lori must work for narrators she has never met. The locked-narrator framing in the prose below is reclassified as a temporary operating constraint, not an architectural commitment.

Interview default is moving from questionnaire-first to **oral-history-as-default** (narrator tells chapters; Lori listens and follows). Structured styles become operator-selectable overrides of that default.

**Read before starting any new work:**

- [`docs/architecture/HORNELORE-UNIVERSAL-PIVOT-STRATEGY.md`](docs/architecture/HORNELORE-UNIVERSAL-PIVOT-STRATEGY.md) — strategy ADR (who Lori is for)
- [`docs/architecture/LORI-RUNTIME-ARCHITECTURE.md`](docs/architecture/LORI-RUNTIME-ARCHITECTURE.md) — nine-stage runtime pipeline (how Lori's behavior is produced)
- [`docs/architecture/MEMORY-EXERCISE-DECISION.md`](docs/architecture/MEMORY-EXERCISE-DECISION.md) — memory_exercise style is kept; implementation WO is parked
- [`docs/architecture/COWORK-HANDOFF.md`](docs/architecture/COWORK-HANDOFF.md) — the operational brief that landed the pivot

Active WO specs live in `docs/wo/`. The 6 live WO specs for the next build sessions:

## Mission

Lorevox is a privacy-first conversational memory system that helps older adults preserve life stories, supports cognitive engagement, and provides structured legacy outputs for family. The broader goal is a digital companion for aging populations — supporting memory recall, emotional processing, and intergenerational storytelling.

**This north star is more important than any single WO.** When a decision trades operational tidiness against narrator dignity, narrator dignity wins. The narrator is not an interview subject, not a data source, not a knowledge graph to populate — the narrator is the author of their own story, and the system exists to help them tell it. Operator-side tooling, eval harnesses, and extractor improvements all serve that. None of them outrank it.

**Hornelore** is the working implementation Chris uses with his own family (Kent and Janice Horne, his parents). The patterns shipped here graduate to **Lorevox**, the public product.

## Design principles (locked)

- **No dual metaphors.** Life Map is the only navigation surface. The river metaphor (Kawa, Memory River) was a useful theoretical lens early; it's retired as system, UI, and logic. Kept as a research citation only.
- **No operator leakage.** Anything a narrator can see or interact with must be designed for narrators. No Return-to-Operator buttons, no diagnostic surfaces, no operator-only controls in the narrator flow. Every UI element passes a role check.
- **No system-tone outputs.** Anything visible to the narrator sounds like a person talking, not a database query result. "(not on record yet)" disappears in narrator-facing output. "Based on: interview projection, session notes" never reaches the narrator. Source-of-truth attribution is operator-side.
- **No partial resets.** Reset Identity clears all narrator-scoped state in one operation, atomically. No lingering memoir cache, no surviving runtime softened-mode, no localStorage remnants. If a reset doesn't reset everything, it isn't done.

- **Provisional truth persists. Final truth waits for the operator. The interview never waits.** Extraction candidates become provisional truth at the moment of extraction — written to BB with `status: "provisional"` and full provenance (source utterance, confidence, timestamp, extractor version). Provisional truth is what Lori reads from in-session AND across sessions; it persists to the server-side narrator record without any approval gate. HITL review is asynchronous and operator-side — a separate review surface (Bug Panel queue, dedicated review dashboard) the operator visits on their own time to promote provisional → confirmed or reject → discard. **Inline review widgets that interrupt the narrator interview are retired.** The reference example of what this principle prevents: TEST-23 v1+v2 (2026-05-04) showed Mary's identity (name + POB) vanishing across browser restart because extraction candidates sat in an in-session shadow-review queue that evaporated on close. Lori knew the values mid-session via chat history; the BB writes never escaped review; the queue cleared on restart; the narrator effectively forgot her own name. That class of failure is exactly what provisional-as-default eliminates.

- **Lorevox is the memory system; Lori is the conversational interface to it.** Memory, structure, chronology, and context belong in the DB schema, the timeline render, and operator-curated context packs — not in Lori's head. Lori's job is to listen, reflect, ask, connect, and follow. Letting Lori carry memory generation, history contextualization, or structural reasoning destabilizes her — that path leads back toward the failure modes Lorevox explicitly avoids: chatbot memoir generator, fake historian, therapist simulator, identity classifier. The system carries the load so Lori can do what she's actually good at. Locked 2026-05-05 alongside the WO-TIMELINE-RENDER-01 + WO-TIMELINE-CONTEXT-EVENTS-01 pair; the architecture follows from this principle (DB/schema = memory + context, Timeline = visual scaffold, Lori = human-feeling conversational layer).

- **Mechanical truth must visibly project.** Any value that exists in canonical or provisional truth (`profile_json`, `projection_json`, promoted/confirmed `story_candidates`, `photos`, `timeline_context_events`) must mirror visibly into the surfaces that consume it — Lori's runtime knowledge via `_build_profile_seed`, the BB UI mirror via projection-sync, the timeline render via the read endpoint, the memoir export via the timeline JSON. Hidden state, inferred reconstruction, and "Lori remembers" pattern-completion are forbidden. The reference example: Mary v6 (2026-05-05) — her identity persisted server-side after Phase A landed but BB state still showed `firstName=None` because the projection→BB mirror was missing. The narrator effectively appeared identity-less in the operator-facing surface even though Lori knew her name fine. Phase E of WO-PROVISIONAL-TRUTH-01 closes that gap; this principle prevents future surfaces from re-introducing it. Pairs with principle 5 (persistence) and principle 6 (ownership) — those cover *what* truth is and *who owns it*; this principle covers *whether the surfaces that need it can see it*.

- **Operator seeds known structure; Lori reflects what is there.** Known narrator facts, family structure, locations, dates, and timeline context belong in BioBuilder/profile truth, projection truth, and operator-curated context packs. Lori must not interrogate the narrator for facts the system already has. **If the operator seeded it, Lori knows it. If Lori knows it, she does not ask for it as intake.** If the fact is not seeded, Lori may learn it naturally when the narrator volunteers it. **Live Lori sessions must not auto-advance questionnaire fields.** Reference example: the questionnaire-first lane was the system's first attempt at structured-field capture; it became Lori interrogating the narrator mid-conversation for fields the operator could enter directly in BB, racing memory_echo + corrections + Life Map era-clicks for turn ownership, and intentionally suppressing the existing welcome-back composer (`hornelore1.0.html:5179` `_ssIsQF` gate) so the QF lane could own first-prompt dispatch on narrator switch — which is what produced Mary's and Marvin's cold-start "Hi, I'm Lori" greetings on switch-back instead of "Welcome back" continuation. WO-QUESTIONNAIRE-FIRST-RETIRE-LIVE-01 retires that behavior on the live narrator path. This principle prevents future surfaces — structured-intake-mode, era-prompt walks, timeline-context capture loops, kinship-skeleton confirmation, anything else that builds a "next field to ask" loop — from re-introducing interrogation under a different name. Pairs with principle 6 (Lorevox is the memory system; Lori is the conversational interface to it) — principle 6 covers *what work Lori does*; this principle covers *what work Lori does NOT do because the system already did it*. Pairs with principle 7 (visible projection) — principle 7 says operator-seeded values must reach Lori's runtime; this principle says once they have, Lori must use them as known, not re-discover them.

These eight principles are checked against every UI element, every data write, and every WO acceptance criterion.

**Canonical extractor architecture reference:** `docs/specs/LOREVOX-EXTRACTOR-ARCHITECTURE-v1.md`. Consult this when scoping any extractor-lane WO, prompt experiment, or eval. Core Law: *Extraction is semantics-driven, but errors arise from failures in causal attribution at the binding layer.* Five-layer pipeline: Architectural / Control / Binding (primary failure surface) / Decision / Evaluation. Type A/B/C question typology LOCKED.

## Travel Doc Evidence + Web Context Rule (permanent doctrine, 2026-07-10)

Travel Doc mode is the OPERATOR memoir-building workspace — not Narrator
Room / dementia-safe life-story mode. Narrator Room stays cautious (no
surprise machine guesses, no raw-metadata overload, gentle and human).
Travel Doc is EVIDENCE-RICH: use all available evidence (EXIF/filename
dates, GPS + reverse-geocoded broad place, OCR, draft image observations,
captions, operator/approved notes, trip route hierarchy, prior notes,
modal captures, and web/public context) to build the best travelogue,
with provenance wording.

**The rule is not "no web." The local Hornelore LLM/API may use web and
public-context tools in Travel Doc mode** (holidays, local events, museum
and site background, food context, neighborhood context, reverse
geocoding). The boundary is: **do not outsource private narrator memory
archives, life-story profiles, or raw memoir transcripts to an
uncontrolled cloud LLM as the reasoning engine.** Local web-enabled
evidence enrichment is allowed; cloud life-story outsourcing is not.
Web-derived context must be labeled as public context or draft evidence
until confirmed by the operator/narrator, and public context is never
presented as personal memory.

## Environment

- **OS**: Windows 11 + WSL2 (Ubuntu). Chris works from WSL.
- **Repo path (WSL)**: `/mnt/c/Users/chris/hornelore` — NOT `~/hornelore`.
- **Agent workspace mount**: `/sessions/<session-id>/mnt/hornelore`. Edits here are live on Chris's repo.
- **Git is NOT accessible from the sandbox mount.** `git status`, `git add`, `git commit`, `git diff --stat`, `git log` from the sandbox either fault with "not a git repository" or "unable to read <oid>". This is permanent — do not retry. File reads and edits work; only the git tooling is broken. All commit/branch/log operations must be handed to Chris as copy-paste blocks that he runs from `/mnt/c/Users/chris/hornelore`.
- **Chris commits from the WSL command line, then pushes from GitHub Desktop.** DO produce copy-paste `git add` + `git commit` blocks that he runs from `/mnt/c/Users/chris/hornelore` — this is the intended workflow. Rules for those blocks: stage with specific file paths only (NEVER `git add -A` or `git add .`), one `git add` + `git commit` pair per logical commit, and use `-m` for the subject plus a second `-m` for the body when the change wants one. Do NOT include `git push` in the block — after committing, Chris checks GitHub Desktop for a clean tree and pushes from there. `git status` / `git diff --stat` / `git log` copy-paste blocks are also fine (read-only inspection). Do NOT suggest SSH key swaps, `gh auth setup-git`, PAT entry, or any other auth dance — his auth is already wired through GitHub Desktop and is none of the agent's business.
- **Work directly on main; do NOT create feature branches.** Chris is the only developer on this repo and the branch/PR workflow is overkill. Going forward, every commit lands on `main` directly via GitHub Desktop. Do NOT suggest creating a new branch, opening a PR, or any branch-rename workflow. (Branches created earlier — e.g. `feat/operator-narrator-intake-form` from 2026-06-15 — can be deleted locally after they're merged. New work commits straight to main.)
- **GPU**: NVIDIA RTX 50-series (Blackwell). Local LLM serves from this machine.
- **There are TWO virtualenvs and they are not interchangeable.** `.venv` is the TEST venv (fastapi 0.136.1 / starlette 1.0.0) and is what `.venv/bin/python -m unittest tests.<module>` runs in. `.venv-gpu` is the SERVING venv (fastapi 0.135.1 / starlette 0.52.1) and is what the running stack uses. `.venv` was built ad-hoc and has historically been missing production dependencies that some suites need, which surfaces as a confusing failure rather than an obvious one: a missing `PIL` shows up as `ModuleNotFoundError` at collection, and a missing `python-docx` shows up as every memoir-export assertion getting `503 != 200` because the route's own `_DOCX_AVAILABLE` guard (`server/code/api/routers/memoir_export.py:897`) fires first. Neither is a product defect. Fixed 2026-07-27 by installing the pinned versions from `requirements-gpu.txt` into `.venv`: `Pillow==12.2.0`, `python-docx==1.2.0`, `lxml==6.0.2`. After that, `test_memoir_export_security`, `test_memoir_trip_story_lane`, `test_modal_archive_boundary` and `test_memoir_story_wire` run 56/56 green together. There is no `requirements-test.txt`; if a suite fails on an import or on a blanket 503, check `.venv` against `requirements-gpu.txt` before suspecting the code.

## Stack ownership

- **Chris starts and stops the API and full stack himself.** Do NOT include `./scripts/start_all.sh` or `./scripts/stop_all.sh` in copy-paste blocks.
- The API is assumed to be running at `http://localhost:8000` whenever an eval is asked for.
- **Cold boot takes ~4 minutes** — the HTTP listener comes up in ~60–70s but the LLM weights + extractor warmup continue for another 2–3 minutes after that. A `curl /` health check is NOT sufficient; it only proves the socket is listening, not that the extractor can serve a real request in <30s. This is why Chris owns start/stop: the agent-run combined blocks that restart the stack and immediately kick off evals cold-start the first case into a 90s read-timeout (observed on cg_001 during narrative-field r5c, 2026-04-21).
- If Chris ever explicitly asks for a combined restart+eval block, gate the eval behind an extractor-warmup probe (POST a trivial extract and loop until round-trip is <30s), not a bare `curl /` loop.

## Git hygiene gate

**Before any code-changing work starts, the tree must be clean** (`git status` shows nothing uncommitted). "Code-changing work" = edits to `server/`, `scripts/`, `ui/`, or any file the extractor / eval harness reads at runtime. Docs-only sessions are exempt but should still commit before EOD.

If the tree is dirty when new code work is requested, the agent's **first action** is to flag it and produce a copy-paste commit plan — NOT to start the code work. No "I'll commit after" — uncommitted state compounds silently across sessions because the sandbox can't run git, and a dirty tree destroys the bisect surface that every eval-gated patch depends on (today's 13-item pile-up — overnight WO batch + r5e1 reports + Phase 1 instrumentation all tangled — is the codifying example, 2026-04-22).

Agent action when tree is dirty:

1. Ask Chris to paste `git status && git diff --stat` from `/mnt/c/Users/chris/hornelore`.
2. Group changes into logical commits with **code isolated from docs** (so a regressing code change reverts cleanly without undoing doc work, and vice versa).
3. Produce copy-paste commit blocks using specific file paths (never `git add -A` or `git add .`).
4. Wait for Chris to confirm clean state before starting the next code change.

Exception: throwaway probe outputs under `.runtime/` that regenerate on every run. If in doubt, commit.

## Standard eval command (copy-paste ready)

When asked to run or re-run a master eval, emit exactly this block, rotating the output suffix:

```bash
cd /mnt/c/Users/chris/hornelore
./scripts/archive/run_question_bank_extraction_eval.py --mode live \
  --api http://localhost:8000 \
  --output docs/reports/master_loop01_<SUFFIX>.json
grep "\[extract\]\[turnscope\]" .runtime/logs/api.log | tail -40
```

(Eval runner moved from `./scripts/` to `./scripts/archive/` on 2026-04-25 to keep the start/stop folder clean. See `scripts/archive/README.md`.)

The eval script auto-writes `docs/reports/master_loop01_<SUFFIX>.console.txt` next to the JSON — no shell `| tee` needed (was silently producing 0-byte files under WSL pipe-buffer conditions; r4h's empty console triggered the fix on 2026-04-19).

Suffix convention: latest **locked baseline is `r5h`** (2026-04-22, 70/104, v3=41/62, v2=35/62, mnw=2 known). Earlier ladder: `r4h` TURNSCOPE v2 (#72 closed) → `r4i` #67 date-field (R4 floor 55/104) → `r4j` PROMPTSHRINK (measured, not adopted; flag in-tree for SPANTAG Pass 2) → `r5a–r5e1` NARRATIVE-FIELD (r5e1 floor 59/104) → `r5e2` ATTRIBUTION-BOUNDARY (REJECTED, in-tree behind `HORNELORE_ATTRIB_BOUNDARY=1`) → `r5f` SILENT-OUTPUT Phase 1+2 (69/104) → `r5g` #119 turnscope greatGrandparents (null, closed complete-with-caveat) → `r5h` WO-SCHEMA-ANCESTOR-EXPAND-01 Lane 1 trial annotations (current). Next: `r5i` = full Lane 1 (case_033 + case_039) or next extractor-lane patch.

The grep at the end rotates — change the tag to whatever filter is being tested (`turnscope`, `negation-guard`, `R4-E`, etc.) or drop it when not needed.

## Stubborn-pack diagnostic eval (copy-paste ready)

When the master eval moves but we need to know why stubborn cases (the frozen fail set) did or didn't shift, run this alongside. The master above stays the decision gate; this layer is diagnostic only.

```bash
cd /mnt/c/Users/chris/hornelore
HORNELORE_PROMPTSHRINK=1 ./scripts/archive/run_stubborn_pack_eval.py \
  --tag <SUFFIX> \
  --runs 3 \
  --api http://localhost:8000 \
  --master docs/reports/master_loop01_<SUFFIX>.json
```

Writes `docs/reports/stubborn_pack_<SUFFIX>_run{1,2,3}.json` plus a cross-run `stubborn_pack_<SUFFIX>_stability.json` + `.console.txt`. The stability console includes the master topline (when `--master` supplied) and buckets the 15 stubborn cases into stable_pass / stable_fail / unstable with per-case VRAM-GUARD truncation flag, failure-category change count, and field-path shape-change flag across the 3 runs.

Stubborn pack (15 cases, fixed): `case_008, case_009, case_017, case_018, case_053, case_075, case_080, case_081, case_082, case_083, case_084, case_085, case_086, case_087, case_088`.

Drop the `HORNELORE_PROMPTSHRINK=1` prefix when running the legacy prompt path — the wrapper itself is env-flag-agnostic.

## Standard post-eval audit block

After every eval that follows a code change, report this exact block before declaring any movement real:

- total pass count
- v2 contract subset
- v3 contract subset
- must_not_write violations
- named affected cases (newly passed, newly failed)
- pass↔fail flips
- scorer-drift audit on every flip (eyeball the truth zones — does the score change reflect a real extraction change, or a scorer/expectation drift?)

## Chris's working preferences

- Honest critique over flattery. Push back on ideas when warranted.
- Tight readouts, not walls of text.
- Do NOT relitigate things already decided.
- When three agents (Claude/Gemini/ChatGPT) converge on the same answer, act on it; don't re-argue.
- Do not regenerate command blocks from memory — copy from this file.
- Read logs and reports directly from the workspace mount; don't ask Chris to paste.

## Companion stack (Lori — the point of the whole system)

The extraction pipeline is one output surface; **Lori is the companion** — designed around older-adult narrators with possible cognitive decline. When planning UI, interview flow, or LORI-CONFIRM work, remember the narrator may not be able to correct, clarify, or re-engage on a standard cadence.

**WO-10C Cognitive Support Mode (landed, dementia-safe):** six behavioral guarantees — protected silence, invitational re-entry, no correction, single-thread context, visual-as-patience, invitational prompts. Silence timing stretched 30s/55–75s → 120s/300s/600s. Re-entry bypasses confidence gates. **Known gap: no operator UI toggle yet — flag must be set programmatically.** Report: `Hornelore-WO10C-Cognitive-Support-Report.docx`.

**Facial awareness stack (browser-only, zero video transmission):** MediaPipe FaceMesh (468 landmarks) → geometry rules → affect labels (steady / engaged / reflective / moved / distressed / overwhelmed). Never ships video, raw landmarks, or raw emotion vectors — only derived `affect_state` + confidence + duration. `facial-consent.js` persists consent in `localStorage['lorevox_facial_consent_granted']`. `emotion.js` L348 has a load-bearing SIMD→non-SIMD WASM redirect (SIMD build crashes at `loadGraph` on Chris's stack). `cognitive-auto.js` v7.4C policy: visual can *accelerate* but not *cause* a mode transition; text has veto.

**Camera / mic / TTS state-machine interactions:** activation chain is `toggleEmotionAware()` → `FacialConsent.request()` → `LoreVoxEmotion.init()` → `LoreVoxEmotion.start()` → `cameraActive=true` + `state.inputState.cameraActive=true` + `window.lv74.showCameraPreview()`. Two truth sources for `cameraActive` (global at `state.js:407` and mirror at `state.js:283`) can desync on narrator switch. Perm-card path flips `emotionAware=true` only — it does NOT call `startEmotionEngine`. `camera-preview.js` reuses the emotion-engine's hidden video `srcObject`; if absent, falls back to a second `getUserMedia` (can double-prompt or fail silently). WO-MIC-UI-02A's 4-state visual (LISTENING / OFF / WAIT amber / BLOCKED) × WO-10H turn-claim state machine × WO-10C stretched silence can interact badly when TTS on 8001 errors mid-stream (mic stuck amber).

**Diag panel first.** For any camera/mic/TTS bug, open the in-app diag at `app.js:5730–5819` (`lv10dSyncHeaderControls`) — it already emits warnings for "Camera active but preview DOM not created", "emotionAware=true but facial consent declined", "Turn state stuck in awaiting_tts_end", plus live `lv10dBpFacialConsent` / `lv10dBpConsentStored` / `lv10dBpCamPreview` / `lv10dBpTts` / `lv10dBpSignalAge` readouts. Check these first; the fault surface collapses to one branch.

**Parallel/supporting subsystems:**

- **Kawa (RETIRED 2026-05-01)** — the river metaphor was a useful theoretical lens early on; gave the project vocabulary for client-as-theorist framing and "river of memories" visualization. The implementation has converged on the canonical 7-era life spine + Life Map UI, and a second river metaphor confused both the model and the user (the broken "narrator-room Memory River view tab" in the 2026-04-30 audit was the trigger). Decision: **Life Map is the only navigation surface; Memory River is removed as a UI.** Kawa is kept as a research citation only — the four papers in `Research/Kawa/` (OTI2023-2768898, TST.2024.9010104, The_Dynamic_Use_of_the_Kawa_Model_A_Scop, newbury-lape-2021-well-being-aging-in-place) support the academic framing of "narrator-as-theorist of their own life" for write-ups. No Kawa engine, no Kawa rendering, no River-of-Memories pre-generation.

- **Pheno (PARKED)** — lived experience + wisdom extraction, separate from truth fields. DESIGN COMPLETE spec, not wired. Doesn't conflict with anything currently shipping. Reactivate when extractor lane settles.

- **WO-STT-LIVE-02 fragile-fact transcript guard** is landed (7-pattern classifier + 30s staleness cap + typed-input fallback).

## Changelog

- 2026-07-27 (**BUG-LORI-REASONING-LEAK-01 -- Lori's own planning sentence reached the transcript, and the fix is one regex because the guard that should have caught it was already running**): A live turn was persisted as assistant content reading `The narrator is speaking in English, so I will respond in English too.` followed by the real greeting in quotes. The instinct is to suspect the archive or the Travel Doc modal wiring; both are wrong. `apply_response_guards` is called at `chat_ws.py:4375`, after comm_control and the reflection-shaper finalize the text and before persist and the WS done event, and that call site sits upstream of the modal archive skip -- so the guard did run on this turn. What failed was `detect_meta_response_leak()`, which had no pattern for either shape in that sentence: `_META_PREAMBLE_RX` enumerates `I will respond BY/WITH/USING` and the leak said `respond IN English`, and nothing anywhere matched a third-person `The narrator is ..., so I ...` planning clause. Two alternatives were added to `_META_REASONING_RX`, both deliberately narrow -- the language branch is pinned to an explicit language vocabulary so prose like `he never knew how to respond in a crisis` cannot trip it, and the third-person branch is anchored on the `, so I` tail so a passing mention of the word narrator is not enough. No repair function was touched and none needed to be: `_META_REASONING_RX` already drives per-sentence removal inside `repair_meta_response_leak`, so dropping the planning sentence leaves the quoted greeting, which the existing `_QUOTED_DRAFT_RX` branch recovers -- the narrator's real `Hi there, I'm Lori...` rather than the `Tell me more about that.` fallback. Files changed: `server/code/api/services/lori_response_guards.py` (one regex, +24 lines, most of it the comment bounding each branch). Files added: `docs/wo/BUG-LORI-REASONING-LEAK-01_Spec.md`; `tests/boris_quality/test_phase5_meta_response_guard.py` gains `ReasoningLeakGuardTests` (7 tests, including the verbatim 2026-07-27 string as the fixture, four false-positive controls asserted byte-identical after repair, and a test that fails if anyone rewrites the repair path or smuggles a bug-specific string into the module). Active baseline unchanged: `chat_ws.py`, guard ordering, `apply_response_guards`, every repair function, every archive and modal surface boundary, export, import-provenance, memoir -- `test_modal_archive_boundary` still 5/5. Flag state: no flag involved, nothing added. Tests: 9 in the Phase 5 suite, 96 across the four guard suites plus the archive boundary, 3 in Phase 6, all OK; `test_chat_ws_guard_failure` needs real fastapi and must run in `.venv`. Open items: live re-observation is opportunistic because the leak is stochastic, and the 2026-07-10 and 2026-07-27 rows stay in the database as history under the WO-2 Decision 4 no-DELETE posture.

- 2026-07-27 (**WO-MEMOIR-TRIP-STORY-LANE-01 -- approved Travel Doc trip stories now reach the narrator memoir DOCX, and they get there by a DB read rather than by rebuilding the archive bridge that the previous work order just refused**): The closing diagnosis of WO-EXPORT-ARCHIVE-WRITER-BRIDGE-01 found the real gap: trip material was captured correctly but had no route into the narrator memoir, because the only route anyone had imagined was archiving `travel_doc_modal` turns as life-story turns -- which is exactly what the two-surface rule of 2026-07-09 forbids. This work order builds the sanctioned route instead. `_trip_story_sections(person_id)` in the memoir export router reads `trip_repository.trip_list(person_id)`, then `location_notes_list(trip_id)` per trip, keeps only rows the operator has explicitly promoted with `include_in_memoir=1` and a non-blank `note_text`, and emits one `MemoirSection` per trip labelled `From your travels -- <trip title>` so the provenance is legible in the finished document. It mirrors the existing `_captured_story_sections` lane exactly, which is why trip material flows through translation and all four DOCX renderers with zero renderer changes. The lane is gated on `HORNELORE_TRIPS` -- trips are a default-OFF surface, so if the operator has not enabled them the memoir does not quietly grow a travel chapter either. Dated trips run chronologically and undated ones trail. It never raises: an unreadable repository logs and returns `[]`, an unreadable trip logs and skips only that trip, because a memoir export must not fail over trip rows. Hidden notes were already excluded by `location_notes_list`'s default and a test asserts the lane never passes `include_hidden=True`. The new `include_trip_stories` request field defaults to True and lets a caller opt out. Files changed: `server/code/api/routers/memoir_export.py` (+98 lines: one request field, one harvester, one append block in `api_memoir_export_docx`). Files added: `tests/test_memoir_trip_story_lane.py` (326 lines, 21 tests in 6 classes), `docs/wo/WO-MEMOIR-TRIP-STORY-LANE-01_Spec.md`. Active baseline unchanged: no UI file, no schema, no migration, no `ui/js/archive-writer.js`, no `chat_ws.py`, no trip DOCX export path -- `trips.py:2099` still renders from `trip_memoir_preview()` exactly as before, and a test proves it. Flag state: no new flag; `HORNELORE_TRIPS` still default-OFF, `HORNELORE_MEMOIR_EXPORT_ENABLED` unchanged, `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE=1` unchanged in `.env`. Three tests in `TwoSurfaceBoundaryTest` exist specifically to keep this lane honest: the module contains no `append_event` call, the harvester's executable body (AST-unparsed with the docstring stripped, so prose cannot mask a real call) mentions `trip_repository` and never `archive`, and `tests/test_modal_archive_boundary.py` still passes all five of its own tests unchanged. Suites green on the device VM: 21 trip-lane, 5 modal-boundary, 9 memoir-story-wire. `tests/test_memoir_export_security` imports real fastapi and must be run in `.venv` -- that run is the one open item, along with a live export against a promoted note. Open items also carried forward: no operator has ever used the `In memoir` toggle that `travel-doc-lab.js:5588` has been rendering all along, so all twelve `trip_location_notes` rows still sit at `include_in_memoir=0` and this lane will correctly produce nothing until one is promoted; Chris ruled the operator promotion surface `later, but useful`. The Lori reasoning leak is next as its own small bug work order.

- 2026-07-27 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01 Phase 4 -- the live smoke ran, 26 of 27 probes passed, and the 27th found the exact hole Decision 4 refused a DELETE route over. Decision 5 closes it in the repository rather than in the screen**): The smoke was driven from the `:8082` operator page against the API on `:8000` -- the **serving** stack, deliberately not the unittest harness, because `.venv` and `.venv-gpu` disagree on FastAPI and Starlette and the serving one is what answers real requests; the flag came from `.env` rather than an interactive `export`, which never reaches a stack started from a shortcut. **What held, all twenty-six of them:** `POST /batches` and five `POST /batches/{id}/candidates`; the `/queue` shape carrying `total`, `returned`, `state_counts` and `queue_depth`; `PATCH /candidates/{id}/trip` filing to the Bismarck trip, and **409** with the candidate named when filed to a bogus trip id; hiding one candidate taking the queue 5 -> 4 and `include_hidden=true` bringing it back to 5, then unhide restoring it; `rejected`, `duplicate` and `error` decisions all 200; **409** on `accepted` with no `photo_id`, with a message that says why; **400** on the state `"skipped"`, *'skipped' is not a decision*; **409** on promote with no file, carrying the full `PhotoBytesMissing` explanation; **415** on `text/plain`; a real JPEG promoting **200** with `created: true`; the candidate still **`pending`** afterwards, which is build point 3; the re-promote returning the same `photo_id` with `created: false, reused: "candidate"`; `accepted` **with** the `photo_id` returning 200; and **405 on all three DELETE probes** -- candidate, batch and decision. The promoted `photos` row was read straight out of SQLite rather than trusted from the response and is born exactly as Phase 3 promised: `narrator_ready=0`, `date_approved_for_lori=0`, `location_approved_for_lori=0`, `deleted_at=NULL`, owned by the candidate's person and nobody else's. **Blast radius, fingerprinted read-only before and after:** `photos` 16 -> 17, `import_batch` 0 -> 1, `import_candidate` 0 -> 5, with `trips` 2, `people` 36 and `trip_photo_context` 51 all unmoved. Residue was retired with `hidden` rather than deleted, which is the doctrine Decision 4 wrote down. **The one probe that failed:** re-deciding an already-`accepted` candidate as `rejected` returned **200** and set its `photo_id` to NULL, leaving the promoted `photos` row unreferenced, still unapproved and unreachable from the queue -- *the identical failure mode Decision 4 refused DELETE over*, open all along one route to the left. **Why nothing caught it:** the invariant was asserted twice and enforced zero times -- once in `candidate_decide()`'s own docstring, once in the screen's comment (*"this screen does not re-open a decision"*) -- and the only thing actually holding it was the UI's `c.state === "pending"` gate in `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js`. A screen is not an invariant, and a `grep` of the server suites found **zero** tests on the behaviour in either direction. The live candidate was un-stranded through the sanctioned route before the smoke closed. **Chris's ruling, verbatim:** *candidate_decide() may only decide a pending candidate. If current state is accepted, rejected, duplicate or error, then any new decision returns 409 and changes nothing* -- and on placement, *"The guard belongs in `import_repository.candidate_decide()`, not only in the UI. The UI already hides re-decision, but WO-3 Picker/Takeout or any future caller could hit the API directly."* **What was built:** a new `CandidateAlreadyDecidedError` beside `BatchClosedError`, mapped to **409 and not 400** -- a deliberate deviation from the option text, which said `InvalidStateError` / 400, because the payload is well formed and `rejected` is a real decision; it is this candidate's state that refuses it, which is precisely the `BatchClosedError` precedent, and a 400 would send the caller off to fix a request with nothing wrong with it. The guard sits immediately after `_candidate_row()` and **before** `_assert_photo_owned_by()`, so a refused re-decision performs no lookups and no writes at all, and it tests **stored state** rather than whether the new decision differs from the old, because re-asserting the *same* decision is still a second review event and would still rewrite `reviewed_by_user_id` and `reviewed_at` over the first. **A mirror guard was added to `candidate_promote()` and it goes beyond Chris's literal answer, which is flagged rather than buried:** a candidate already `rejected` / `duplicate` / `error` with **no** photo could still be promoted, minting a `photos` row the new decide guard then guarantees can never be referenced, since that candidate can no longer be accepted; it is conditioned on `not cand.get("photo_id")` so the duplicate-click promote+accept retry still answers 200 with the same id, and reverting it is one `if` block plus three tests. **Tests -- and one whose intent was deliberately inverted:** `test_rejecting_after_promoting_unlinks_but_keeps_the_photo` was written to *lock* the old 200-and-clear behaviour as "a known and accepted consequence", and Chris's ruling reverses it, so it was rewritten rather than deleted and narrowed to the case that is still true -- deciding a **pending** promoted candidate as `rejected` does clear the link, and that is a legitimate first decision -- as `test_rejecting_a_promoted_but_still_pending_candidate_unlinks_it`, with the rewrite called out in its own comment so it cannot read as drift. It is **not** one of the no-DELETE tests, so Decision 4's *"do not change the no-DELETE tests"* does not cover it, and `PromoteAddsNoDeleteTests`, `test_hide_is_reversible_and_the_queue_has_no_delete`, the route-count gate and `test_decided_rows_are_not_re_decidable_from_this_screen` are all untouched. Added: `DecisionsAreOneWayTests` (13) covering `accepted -> rejected` 409, the accepted row keeping its `photo_id`, its `state_reason`, its reviewer and its `reviewed_at`, **the whole candidate row compared before and after so a partial write cannot hide**, `rejected -> accepted` / `duplicate -> rejected` / `error -> accepted` all 409, re-asserting the same decision 409, `pending` still taking all four decisions, and the `photos` row byte-identical after a refusal, still `narrator_ready=0` and not soft-deleted; and `PromotingADecidedCandidateTests` (3) for the mirror guard. On the router side, a route-level 409 test on the wire, the new error added to `test_every_repository_error_has_a_deliberate_status`, and a separately named test pinning **409 specifically** rather than merely sub-500, because which code it gets is the decision. **Not a defect, written down so the next reader stops where I stopped:** `stored_rejected_count` reading 2 against a live `state_counts` of `rejected: 1` is not counter drift -- `_refresh_batch_counters()` recomputes rather than increments and deliberately groups `rejected + duplicate` into one stored number, while the queue reports the five states separately. Two groupings on one response, both correct. **Route surface unchanged at 17 routes, still zero DELETE** -- Decision 5 adds an error class and a guard, not a route, so `test_route_count_is_the_count_the_gate_test_covers` is untouched. **Flagged, not fixed, all outside this lane:** the on-disk archive store under `DATA_DIR/memory/archive/people/<person_id>/` has had nothing written to it since **2026-07-23 22:59** while the `turns` table kept recording normally (07-24: 18, 07-25: 2, 07-26: 2, 07-27: 4) -- the cause is named in the browser console, `[archive-writer] auto-chain DISABLED (BUG-209: backend chat_ws is sole transcript writer)`, and the travel-doc Lori surface writes turns without ever calling `archive.append_event()`, so **nothing is lost** and the fix belongs to the Export lane, epic item 5; Lori's reply leaked its own reasoning into the **persisted** turn content (*"The narrator is speaking in English, so I will respond in English too"* followed by the real reply in quotes), confirmed in `turns` rather than a rendering artifact, so it will carry into any memoir or export built from that turn -- WO-5, build point 10; and `lv80ToggleNarratorSwitcher is not defined` fires from a `div` `onclick` in `hornelore1.0.html`. **Files changed:** `server/code/api/services/import_repository.py` (1620 -> 1680), `server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py` (731 -> 736), `tests/test_import_provenance_promote.py` (856 -> 1065), `tests/test_import_provenance_routes.py` (886 -> 915), `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01_Spec.md` (685 -> 890 -- a `## Phase 4` section with the full probe table and a `## Decision 5` section), `CLAUDE.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`. **Files added:** none. **Active baseline: 150 / 78 / 69 / 56** (travel_doc_lab / promote / routes / queue), up from 150 / 62 / 67 / 56; all four green in the sandbox, `ast.parse` clean on both edited modules, and all four code files md5-verified onto the device after delivery. **The live re-smoke RAN AND PASSED 2026-07-27 against the restarted stack**, all seven steps Chris specified, driven from the `:8082` operator page against `:8000`: a fresh `local_upload` batch and candidate, `POST /promote` with real PNG bytes returning **200** with the candidate still `pending`, `POST /decision` accepting with that `photo_id` returning **200**, then the same candidate re-decided as `rejected` returning **409** with the guard's own message, and after the refusal the candidate still `accepted`, `state_reason` still `resmoke accept`, `reviewed_by_user_id` and `reviewed_at` unchanged, `photo_id` still attached, and the **whole candidate row byte-identical before and after** the refused call. The promoted `photos` row was read straight out of SQLite read-only and is born `narrator_ready=0` / `date_approved_for_lori=0` / `location_approved_for_lori=0` / `needs_confirmation=1` / `deleted_at=NULL` -- not narrator-facing and not Lori-approved. A pre-flight probe on the previous run's photo-less rejected candidate confirmed the guard was loaded before any of this touched a promoted row. Blast radius `photos` 17 -> 18, `import_batch` 1 -> 3, `import_candidate` 5 -> 7. Both re-smoke batches retired closed + hidden per Decision 4. **Open items:** the `candidate_promote()` mirror guard is offered for reversion; the archive-writer gap, the Lori reasoning leak and the `lv80ToggleNarratorSwitcher` error are all recorded and all out of scope here; the `.venv` / `.venv-gpu` FastAPI/Starlette drift still matters because the serving venv is what answers requests; `WO-E2E-HARNESS-SANDBOX-01` recorded and not worked; the `trip_bio_suggestions` force-delete-gate question is still Chris's; four vestigial `LV_ENABLE_*` flags still need his call; and the env-flag drift audit remains parked, not cancelled -- 67 code-referenced flags undocumented, an unapplied Deliverable 2 in `ENV_EXAMPLE_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md`, and a helper-indirection defect at eight dynamic `os.getenv(var, ...)` call sites that would make any regex-based drift gate lie. **The scope wall holds: WO-3 Google Photos Picker does not start in this session.**)

- 2026-07-27 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01 Decision 4 -- DELETE was raised, analysed, and refused; the flag moved into `.env`; and the Phase 5 smoke residue is confirmed gone from the database**): Chris asked for a delete route mid-build, which reverses build point 12 of his own Phase 2/3 order, then refined it to the question that actually mattered -- *"if we do not have a delete option what if we make a mistake and want to delete what actually would we delete, can it be a soft delete and also a permenate one"*. The answer was gathered from the codebase rather than from memory, and it changed his mind. **The soft delete already exists and it is called `hidden`** -- `PATCH /candidates/{id}/hidden` and `PATCH /batches/{id}/hidden`, honoured by `queue_read()` in both forms, reversible, recoverable through `include_hidden`, already on the screen as Hide / Unhide, and pinned by eight tests including the one proving that reopening a batch is not the same as unhiding it. A hidden candidate is invisible to the queue and intact in the database, so a second soft delete would have been duplicate machinery under a scarier word. **Mapping every "mistake" onto what already covers it left exactly one gap:** junk that landed is `rejected` or `error`, a whole bad import is a hidden batch, a misfiling is `PATCH /candidates/{id}/trip` -- and the only thing nothing covers is a promotion you regret, because hiding the candidate does not touch the `photos` row that promotion created. That was the honest justification for a delete route and it was the only one. **The two precedents that made "soft and also permanent" a fair ask are real:** `people.py` carries `DELETE /api/people/{person_id}` soft by default and hard with `?mode=hard` plus `POST /api/people/{person_id}/restore`, and `photos.py` carries `DELETE /api/photos/{photo_id}` as a pure soft delete that requires `actor_id` and returns the row read back with `deleted=True`. **The trap decided it:** a permanent delete of a *promoted* candidate must either refuse with a 409 or also deal with the photo, and if it does neither it strands an unreferenced, still-unapproved `photos` row -- the identical failure mode already refused for re-deciding, so DELETE would have re-opened at the operator's fingertips the exact hole the promote design was built to close. **Chris's ruling, verbatim:** do not add DELETE; keep the import/evidence lane reversible through hidden/unhidden; do not change the no-DELETE tests; do not add DELETE to the sanctioned route surface; do not delete candidates from the UI; do not solve smoke residue with a permanent API route; for operator cleanup, `hidden` is the product behaviour, manual DB cleanup with backup remains Chris-only, and a future maintenance/cleanup work order can define a guarded purge tool if needed; and for promoted candidates deletion is especially not allowed because it can strand a `photos` row, so build around visibility / state / audit instead. **The product rule, as he wrote it:** *"Evidence candidates are auditable intake records. Bad or unwanted ones are hidden, rejected, duplicate, or error. They are not deleted from the operator UI."* **Screen wording rule:** operator-facing labels use Hide, Unhide, Retire from queue, or Remove from review view -- never *Delete*. **Audited rather than assumed:** `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js` was grepped case-insensitively and the Evidence panel's complete label set is Refresh, Show/Hide hidden, Unhide, Promote + accept, Reject, Duplicate, Error, File to trip, Hide, Restore, Save placement and Cancel, with **zero** occurrences of "Delete"; every `Delete` string in that file belongs to the gated trip force-delete ladder and the region/stop review drawers from WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 3A, which is a different subsystem and the only sanctioned DELETE the Lab issues. **No code change was needed to comply, so none was made.** **The route surface is unchanged at 17 routes, still zero DELETE** -- verified by counting `@router.*` decorators in `server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py` (17, of which 0 are `delete`) against the entries in `FlagGateTests._all_routes()` (17), which is exactly the equality `test_route_count_is_the_count_the_gate_test_covers` exists to hold. **`PromoteAddsNoDeleteTests` (3 tests), `test_hide_is_reversible_and_the_queue_has_no_delete`, and the route-count gate stay exactly as written** -- they assert an absence, and that absence now has a name and a rationale instead of being an unexplained gap a future session might helpfully fill in. **`.env` changed (232 -> 239 lines), backed up first to `.env.bak-pre-import-provenance-20260727`:** `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE=1` inserted after `HORNELORE_TRIPS=1`, mirroring `.env.example` ordering, because Chris starts the stack from a shortcut and an `export` in an interactive shell never reaches it -- `main.py:37-40` does `load_dotenv(str(_env_file), override=False)`, so shell env still wins where it is set and the `.env` value applies otherwise. Both `.env` and the backup are gitignored (`.gitignore:90`) and neither is in this commit; `.env.example:870` still documents the flag at `0` and the code default is still OFF. **I should have suggested `.env` first and did not** -- I had wrongly carried "no `.env` change" forward as a prohibition when it was only a description of what earlier phases happened not to touch, and Chris was right to push back. **Long-standing open item CLOSED -- the two hidden `PHASE5-SMOKE` batches are gone.** A read-only `sqlite3` query against `C:\hornelore_data\db\hornelore.sqlite3` returns `import_batch` **0 rows**, `import_candidate` **0 rows** and no hidden batches of any label, with `photos` **16**, `trips` **2** and `people` **36** confirming nothing outside the import lane moved. Chris cleared them himself against the database, which is the worked example of the cleanup doctrine Decision 4 has now written down -- residue is an operator action with a backup, not a permanent API route added for the purpose. It also means Phase 4's live smoke starts from a clean, measurable baseline. **Git hygiene gate CLOSED:** Chris reported the Phases 1-3 suites green on his own box (150, 62, 67, 56 all OK) and a clean tree after committing. **Files changed:** `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01_Spec.md` (566 -> 685 lines -- a new `## Decision 4` section, and the scope wall gains both the CLOSED note for the smoke batches and a pointer saying the missing DELETE is a decision rather than an oversight), `CLAUDE.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`. **Files added:** none. **Tests:** none changed and none added, because no code changed. **Active baseline unchanged:** 150 / 62 / 67 / 56, as Chris ran them. **Open items:** Phase 4, the live smoke against the serving stack from the operator UI origin, is the only open phase of WO-2 and it is blocked on the stack, which is confirmed DOWN -- `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` on both 8082 and 8000 from the browser -- so Chris starts it from his shortcut, cold boot is about four minutes and a `curl /` health check is not sufficient; the residue question that blocked Phase 4 three times is now answered by doctrine rather than by a route, so a full smoke may leave a batch, its candidates and `photos` 16 -> 17 behind, cleared afterwards by `hidden` or by Chris against the database with a backup; the `.venv` / `.venv-gpu` FastAPI/Starlette drift still matters because the serving venv is what answers requests; `WO-E2E-HARNESS-SANDBOX-01` recorded and not worked; the `trip_bio_suggestions` force-delete-gate question is still Chris's; four vestigial `LV_ENABLE_*` flags still need his call; and the env-flag drift audit remains parked, not cancelled -- 67 code-referenced flags undocumented, an unapplied Deliverable 2 in `ENV_EXAMPLE_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md`, and a helper-indirection defect at eight dynamic `os.getenv(var, ...)` call sites that would make any regex-based drift gate lie. **The scope wall is back in force: the Phase 2+3 override was for that slice only, and WO-3 Google Photos Picker does not start in the same session as Phase 4.**

- 2026-07-27 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01 Phases 3 and 2 -- promotion, then the screen. The Evidence Review Queue is visible and usable in Travel Documenter, and two collisions that would have shipped silently were caught before delivery**): Chris closed all three open decisions and opened Phases 2 and 3 together as one vertical slice -- **the single, explicit override of the standing "a phase is a scope wall" rule, granted for this slice only and not generalisable to the next one.** **The rulings, verbatim in effect:** placement stays **trip-level, no migration 0038**; `changed` and `skipped` **do not become states**; the `photos` row is materialized by an **explicit promote route, option B**. **Phase 3 was built before Phase 2 on purpose** -- Decision 3 means a candidate has no `photo_id` until something promotes it, so "accept" is literally unreachable from a screen until the promote route exists, and shipping an accept button with nothing behind it is worse than shipping nothing. **Phase 3:** `candidate_promote()` appended to `import_repository.py` and `POST /api/import-provenance/candidates/{id}/promote` appended to the router, taking an optional multipart `file`, resolving in a fixed first-match-wins order -- existing `photo_id`, then a declared `file_hash` matching a live photo **of this person**, then sha256 of supplied bytes (a person-scoped hit links, a **global** clash raises `CrossPersonError` 409 rather than quietly linking one narrator's photo into another's queue), then `PhotoBytesMissingError` 409 rather than inventing a photo. Three properties carry the phase and each has tests: the photo is **born not narrator-facing and not Lori-approved** (`_assert_born_unapproved` is a runtime assertion, not a comment -- intake is not approval); **promotion does not decide**, the candidate is still `pending` afterwards and the existing decision route remains the only thing that writes a state; and **promotion is idempotent**, which is the property that makes the halfway state safe to retry and therefore makes the screen's retry message true rather than hopeful. Surface **16 -> 17 routes, still zero DELETE**; no new dependency, `python-multipart` is already in both virtualenvs. **Phase 2:** the **Evidence** tab in Travel Documenter, between Photos and Story Notes, written in that module's existing idiom -- the single `api()` fetch choke point, the single `renderAll()` repaint entry point, `el`/`btn`/`field`/`drawerShell`, in-panel drawers and **no native `prompt`/`confirm`/`alert`**. It shows candidates, the state counts, the batch, the trip **with its date window** (next to `taken_at` deliberately: *does this photo fall inside the trip it is filed under* is the commonest review question and the queue already returns the dates), filename **and** external id both, `match_reason` printed key-by-key exactly as the importer stored it and never paraphrased, and `state` with its reason. Seven row actions: promote + accept, reject, duplicate, error, hide/unhide, file to trip. **Promote + accept is two requests in that order** through one choke point, and if the promote lands and the accept does not the operator is told in those words -- which photo id exists, that the candidate is still pending, and that running it again is safe. **TWO COLLISIONS FOUND BEFORE DELIVERY, NEITHER OF WHICH WOULD HAVE RAISED AN ERROR, both found by enumerating every symbol the new section declares against the rest of the file rather than by reading it.** (1) The first draft used the CSS prefix `tdl-ev-`, which was **already owned** by the per-photo evidence panel from `WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-TOOLS-01` -- `.tdl-ev-off`, `.tdl-ev-row`, `.tdl-ev-summary` and `.tdl-ev-actions` all exist there, and `.tdl-ev-off` in particular is a small inline badge state where mine is a full block, so shipping it would have restyled a working panel from across the file. Renamed to **`tdl-erq-`** by a scoped rewrite over the new lines only, with boundary assertions. (2) `renderEvidenceRow` was **already a module-scope function** at line 4470, called twice; a second module-scope declaration of that name **hoists and replaces the first with no error**, so the photo evidence panel would simply have started rendering candidate rows. Renamed to **`renderErqRow`**. **ONE EXISTING ASSERTION WAS DELIBERATELY WIDENED, and it was left to fail first so the widening is a recorded decision rather than a silent one:** `test_travel_doc_lab.py::test_endpoints_are_a_subset_of_the_sanctioned_api` held a four-entry allow-list (`/api/trips`, `/api/photos/`, `/api/people`, `/api/chat/ws`) and `/api/import-provenance` is now a fifth. It is not a weakening -- the gate is a **prefix allow-list**, so widening it admits exactly one lane and still fails the build on anything else; every route behind that prefix is behind a default-off flag, none is narrator-facing, and none can delete. What the gate exists to catch, this module quietly growing a reach into narrator or Travels-shelf state, is unchanged. **ONE OTHER BOUNDARY CHANGE, also not quiet:** the Evidence tab is **exempt from `renderAll()`'s selected-trip gate and is the only tab that is** (`if (!st.trip && st.tab !== "evidence")`) -- every other tab describes a trip, this one describes a *person's* imports, and the rows most in need of review are precisely the ones not filed to a trip yet, so gating it on a selection would hide the unfiled queue behind a trip the operator has not made. **Decided rows are not re-decidable from this screen** -- a refusal, not an omission, and the row says so: `candidate_decide` writes `photo_id` unconditionally, so re-deciding an accepted candidate would set it to NULL and strand the `photos` row it pointed at, unreferenced and still unapproved. **With the flag off the tab renders its own configuration panel, not the red error bar**, because on a stock checkout that 404 is the correct state and painting it as an error sends an operator hunting for a broken trip. **Known limitation, recorded rather than papered over:** `GET /queue` has no "trip is null" filter, so the unfiled count in the header is computed from the current page and is labelled "on this page" for exactly that reason; unfiled-only belongs in the route, not in client-side guesswork, and the route is a landed phase. **Files changed:** `server/code/api/services/import_repository.py` (`candidate_promote()` appended, no existing function touched), `server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py` (`POST /candidates/{id}/promote` appended), `tests/test_import_provenance_routes.py` (one `FlagGateTests._all_routes()` entry -- required, not cosmetic), `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js` (5788 -> 6432 lines, eight patch sites, no existing function body rewritten), `ui/css/travel-doc-lab.css` (664 -> 713 lines, appended only), `tests/test_travel_doc_lab.py` (`EvidenceReviewQueueTest` added plus the one widened assertion above), `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01_Spec.md`. **Files added:** `tests/test_import_provenance_promote.py`. **Tests:** `test_travel_doc_lab` **129 -> 150** (the 21 new ones map onto Chris's numbered build list rather than onto the code's own shape, and the promote-ordering test is structural -- it asserts the index of `"/promote"` is less than the index of `"/decision"`, because a test that merely asserted both strings are present would pass on code that accepts before it promotes); `test_import_provenance_promote` **62 new**; `test_import_provenance_routes` **67**, unchanged; `test_import_provenance_queue` **56**, unchanged. All green in the sandbox slice before anything reached the device. the promote route sits behind the same router-level dependency as the other 16 and answers 404 rather than 403 when off. **Nothing narrator-facing changed, no Lori behaviour changed, no Google Picker, no Takeout parser, no migration, no `main.py` change, no schema, no `.env`, and no DELETE anywhere on the lane** -- Chris's build points 8 through 12, each with a test. **Open items:** Phase 4, the live smoke against the serving stack from the operator UI origin, is the only phase of WO-2 still open, and it is the phase that matters most here because `.venv` and `.venv-gpu` disagree on FastAPI/Starlette and the serving one is what answers requests; **the git hygiene gate is still open -- Chris has not reported a green test run or a commit for Phase 1 since hitting the missing-`cd` error, so Phase 1 and this slice may want to land as separate commits in order**; the two hidden `PHASE5-SMOKE` batches are still in the database, since there is no DELETE route and removing them is Chris's action against `C:\hornelore_data`; the `.venv` / `.venv-gpu` drift is still a later harness/environment work order per Chris; `WO-E2E-HARNESS-SANDBOX-01` recorded and not worked; the `trip_bio_suggestions` force-delete-gate question is still Chris's; four vestigial `LV_ENABLE_*` flags still need his call; and the env-flag drift audit remains parked, not cancelled -- 67 code-referenced flags undocumented, an unapplied Deliverable 2 in `ENV_EXAMPLE_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md`, and a helper-indirection defect at eight dynamic `os.getenv(var, ...)` call sites that would make any regex-based drift gate lie. **The next phase is a scope wall again: the override was for this slice, and WO-3 Google Photos Picker does not start in the same session as Phase 4.**

- 2026-07-26 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01 Phase 1 -- the queue read. WO-2 of the epic is open, and building it surfaced a structural gap that blocks the screen**): WO-1 built the landing zone and closed green. It can record an import and it can record a decision, but there was no way to look at the pile -- `GET /candidates` is a table read, so a review screen built on it would fetch each candidate's batch and each candidate's trip separately, and a real Takeout import lands hundreds of candidates from a handful of batches. That is an N+1 against the exact shape of the data. **Phase 1 is the page read instead:** the same rows, each carrying inline the batch it arrived in and the trip it is filed under, plus the state counts behind the page, in one query. **Four rules it holds that the raw list does not.** `person_id` is a required argument, not an optional filter -- a review queue with no person is a cross-person read, and the boundary is easier to keep as a required argument than as a caller's discipline; an unknown person **raises** rather than returning `[]`, because *this person has nothing* and *there is no such person* are different facts and must not share an answer. A candidate inside a hidden batch is out of the queue **even when its own `hidden` is 0** -- hiding a batch retires the material it landed, and a queue that kept serving its rows would make batch-hide a lie; `include_hidden=true` restores both kinds and every row carries `batch.hidden` so the caller can tell which it is looking at. `state_counts` covers the whole filtered queue and **deliberately ignores the `state` filter** while `total` honours it: counting only the page would report "12 pending" because twelve fit, counting only the requested state would report the queue depth as the thing you already asked for, and the useful answer is *you are looking at pending, and here is the shape of the whole queue behind it*. And `match_reason` **round-trips verbatim, deep-equal** -- 0037 made that column JSON so the review queue could show the importer's reasoning rather than a summary of it, this is the first caller that displays it, and it must not be the place a summary creeps in. **Order is oldest-first tiebroken on `rowid`**, the Phase 4 lesson applied: `_now()` has whole-second precision, so a whole import lands inside one second and `ORDER BY created_at` alone degrades to uuid order; a twenty-candidate test written inside one second pins it and a second test proves it survives paging. **Join columns are spelled out rather than `SELECT b.*`** because `import_batch`, `import_candidate` and `trips` all carry `id` / `person_id` / `hidden` / `created_at` / `updated_at` and a star-join would let one silently shadow another; one test asserts no `_b_`/`_t_` alias reaches the payload and one asserts the candidate's own `id` is not the batch's. **The trip's date window comes back on purpose** (`start_date` / `end_date`), because the single commonest review question is whether a photo's `taken_at` falls inside the trip it is filed under, and a queue showing only the title would force the reviewer to open the trip to answer it; an unfiled candidate gets `trip: null`, not a dict of nulls, since "not filed yet" is the most common state here and should read as one thing. **It is a read and nothing else** -- four tests assert the database is byte-unchanged across a queue read, that looking at a candidate does not promote it, that the queue materializes no `photos` row, and that it cannot serve a token because intake refuses to store one. **One edit to the routes suite was required and it is not bookkeeping:** `test_route_count_is_the_count_the_gate_test_covers` asserts the route set equals the flag-gate list, so the new route turned that suite red until it was added to `FlagGateTests._all_routes()` -- the guard working exactly as built. It is listed there **without its required `person_id`** on purpose, so the flag-off answer is 404 rather than the 422 that would name the missing parameter and confirm the route exists. **THREE DECISIONS ARE CHRIS'S AND TWO OF THEM BLOCK PHASE 2, the screen -- written up with recommendations in the spec, not guessed at in code.** (1) **Placement granularity:** 0037 has no `proposed_trip_day_id` / `proposed_region_id` / `proposed_stop_id`, so filing is trip-granularity and the route does not invent a finer answer than the schema can hold. *Recommendation: stay there and do not write 0038 yet* -- those columns hold what an **importer** proposed, and there is no importer until WO-3 (Picker) and WO-4 (Takeout), so adding them now commits to a payload shape before its producer exists and leaves three permanently-null columns plus a UI affordance that never fires. 0037 already paid that bill once when the Epic Plan's `review_status` / `operator_decision_json` lost to the shipped names. (2) **`changed` and `skipped`:** neither is a 0037 state and both are refused 400. *Recommendation: neither becomes a sixth state.* `changed` is not a review outcome, it is the difference between what the importer proposed and what the operator saved -- an audit fact that `state_reason` / `reviewed_by_user_id` / `reviewed_at` already hold, and making it a state means a row can be `changed` **or** `accepted` but not both, losing the more important of the two. `skipped` means two things already covered: undecided is `pending`, decided-no is `rejected`; if the real want is *ask me again later*, that is a nullable defer, not a state, because every count and every `decidable_states` check keys on there being exactly five. (3) **NEW, not in the recorded WO-2 prerequisites, and the largest structural gap in this work order: nothing materializes the `photos` row on accept.** `candidate_decide(state='accepted')` requires a `photo_id` pointing at a row that **already exists and is already owned by the candidate's person**, and the repository deliberately never creates one -- that absence is how *intake is not approval* got enforced in Phase 3, with a runtime `PRAGMA table_info` guard behind it. **So the Evidence Review Queue can display, reject, mark duplicate, mark error and file to a trip, and it cannot accept anything.** Phase 5's live smoke only managed an accept because it pointed at one of Christopher's four pre-existing photos. Three options are on the table -- (A) the decision route creates the photo, fewest calls but a write to `photos` inside the decision path, which is the exact boundary Phase 3 was built to hold; (B) an explicit `POST /candidates/{id}/promote` that creates the row from stored metadata, returns its id and leaves the candidate `pending`, so promotion is a separate auditable act; (C) the importer creates the photo at intake born unapproved, simplest but it means intake writes to `photos`, which the 0037 column absences exist to prevent, and it fills the library with rows for material that may never be accepted. *Recommendation: (B)*, the only one that does not weaken a guarantee already shipped and tested. **Honest caveat on (B):** a candidate holds an `external_id` or a filename, **not bytes** -- for local/manual the file is on disk and promotion is a metadata copy, but for Picker or Takeout it needs a real fetch or extract, which is WO-3/WO-4 work, so (B) likely ships in two pieces. **If all three stay undecided, Phase 2 ships a queue that can triage but not approve** -- still most of the work on a several-hundred-candidate Takeout, but that should be a choice made, not a limitation discovered after the screen was built. **Files changed:** `server/code/api/services/import_repository.py` (`queue_read()` + `_QUEUE_BATCH_COLUMNS` + `_QUEUE_TRIP_COLUMNS` appended; no existing function touched), `server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py` (`GET /queue` appended plus three header-docstring edits, since the module's own description still said "this is not the Evidence Review Queue" when part of it now is), `tests/test_import_provenance_routes.py` (the one required `_all_routes()` entry), `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Files added:** `tests/test_import_provenance_queue.py` (56 tests in eight classes), `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-EVIDENCE-REVIEW-QUEUE-01_Spec.md`. **Not touched:** no migration (highest on disk is still 0037, there is no 0038), no `main.py` change (the router was already registered in Phase 4), no UI file, no flag change, no `.env`, no schema, and **no DELETE route on either import lane** -- the surface goes 15 -> 16 routes and stays at zero. **Test baseline 399 -> 455**: the new queue module is 56, and the routes module holds at 67 (the `_all_routes()` entry changes what an existing test asserts, not how many tests there are). Everything was built and run in a sandbox slice of the repo -- `db.py` plus all 36 migrations plus `main.py` plus the two modified modules on **fastapi 0.136.1 / starlette 1.0.0** to match `.venv` rather than the serving `.venv-gpu` -- so both suites were green before anything reached the device. Run them by module name, never by discovery: `unittest discover` cross-contaminates across `tests/` in this repo and pytest is not installed. **Active baseline unchanged:** r5h-followup-guard-v1 (78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2). `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE` still **default-OFF** and the new route is behind the same router-level dependency as the other 15, answering 404 rather than 403 when off. **Open items:** the three decisions above, two of which block Phase 2; the two hidden `PHASE5-SMOKE` batches still in the database, since there is no DELETE route and removing them is Chris's action against `C:\hornelore_data`; the `.venv` / `.venv-gpu` FastAPI/Starlette drift, still a later harness/environment work order per Chris; `WO-E2E-HARNESS-SANDBOX-01` recorded and not worked; the `trip_bio_suggestions` force-delete-gate question still Chris's; and the env-flag drift audit parked, not cancelled -- 67 code-referenced flags undocumented, zero safe deletions, an unapplied Deliverable 2 already sitting in `ENV_EXAMPLE_AUDIT_2026-07-23.md`, and a helper-indirection defect at eight dynamic `os.getenv(var, ...)` call sites that would make any regex-based drift gate lie. **Phase 2, the screen, is a separate session -- a phase is a scope wall.**

- 2026-07-26 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01 Phase 5 -- the live smoke: 66/66 green against the serving stack, and a real flag-gate defect found and fixed on the way**): Phase 4 proved the repository through the backend boundary in a test process. Phase 5 is the only thing that proves it against the code that actually answers requests, and that distinction turned out to matter. **The stack serves under `.venv-gpu` (fastapi 0.135.1 / starlette 0.52.1) while the unittest suite runs under `.venv` (fastapi 0.136.1 / starlette 1.0.0).** Chris's ruling, recorded verbatim because it is the reason this phase looks the way it does: *"The unittest route suite ran under .venv, while the serving API used .venv-gpu with older FastAPI/Starlette. The Phase 5 smoke therefore verified the serving version directly. Dependency alignment belongs in a later harness/environment work order, not inside Import Provenance Phase 5."* So the entire smoke ran over HTTP from the operator UI origin against the running stack -- **66 assertions, 66 pass**. **The defect.** With `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE` off, thirteen of the fifteen routes returned 404 as designed and two did not: `POST /batches` and `POST /candidates/{candidate_id}/decision` answered **422 with the names of their required fields**. FastAPI validates the request body *before* it calls the handler, and the gate was the first line *inside* each handler -- so an empty or malformed body never reached the gate, and a disabled surface disclosed both that it exists and what its schema is. The Phase 4 gate test missed it because `FlagGateTests._all_routes()` only ever sends well-formed bodies; a prober does not. **Fixed in this phase** by promoting the gate from a per-handler call to a **router-level dependency** -- `APIRouter(..., dependencies=[Depends(_require_enabled)])` -- with the per-handler calls kept as belt-and-braces, and confirmed empirically rather than assumed: flag-off now returns 404 for an empty body, a junk body and no body, while flag-on still returns a normal 422 for a genuinely bad one. Two tests added -- every body-taking route x (`{}`, `{"nonsense": true}`, no body) -> 404, and an assertion that `router.dependencies` is non-empty so the gate cannot be quietly demoted back into the handlers. Route suite **65 -> 67**. **The smoke itself.** `/enums` returns exactly the shipped vocabulary and **`skipped` and `changed` are absent, asserted** -- per Chris's correction, the real states are `pending` / `accepted` / `rejected` / `duplicate` / `error`, and the plan's two extra words stay WO-2 design inputs that Phase 4 refuses with 400. Two batches and four candidates in three shapes (Picker-style `external_id`, Takeout-style filename, manual) covered: creation defaults (born `open`, born visible, null trip); person inherited from the batch and never accepted from the caller; oldest-first order stable across equal-second timestamps; `match_reason` round-trip; **four token-shaped inputs refused 400 with the token text absent from the response body and no row created by the refused calls**; accept against an existing same-person photo; reject; duplicate; hide/unhide with decision, reviewer and reason preserved across both; counters; close and reopen with a closed batch refusing new candidates 409; cross-person photo 409 with no partial apply; cross-trip 4xx on batch, candidate and list; and **no DELETE on either lane**. **A deliberate deviation, stated rather than buried:** batch A is Christopher's own person instead of a synthetic one, because all four photos in the database are his and the accept step requires a photo owned by the candidate's person; batch B is Kent's and exists to supply the cross-person case; everything is tagged `PHASE5-SMOKE` and both batches were left `hidden=1`. **The blast radius, proved rather than asserted.** Read-only (`mode=ro`) fingerprints before and after: the `photos` approval subset still hashes `6c4c6a07e2...` and `trip_photo_links` still `e3b0c44298...`, both UNCHANGED; all four photos still carry their April `updated_at`, **including the one the accept pointed at**; `narrator_ready` is still `1,1,0,1`; every `date_approved_for_lori` and `location_approved_for_lori` is still 0; photos / trips / trip_photo_links are still 4 / 0 / 0, so **no route created a photo and no route touched an approval flag**; and zero rows in either import table contain token-shaped text. The full-row `photos` hash `8e5f7a577a...` is recorded as a new forward baseline -- it differs from the approval-subset hash because they cover different columns, not because anything changed. **Two false alarms run to ground instead of reported as defects**, which matters because acting on either would have meant proposing a write against the live database: (1) the single initial FAIL was my assertion, not the product -- `match_reason` is stored via `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)` so keys come back alphabetized, and a deep-equal re-check passes with every key, nested object, float and array element exact; the sorting is deliberate and makes the stored JSON stable and diffable; (2) `stored_rejected_count = 2` against a live `rejected = 1` is correct -- `_refresh_batch_counters` counts `rejected` **and** `duplicate` into the schema's `rejected_count`, and `batch_counts()` returns both the live per-state counts and the stored counters on purpose *"so a caller can see if the two ever disagree"*. They measure different things and do not disagree. **One environment note worth not rediscovering:** Chrome throttles backgrounded tabs, and per-request latency measured through the browser reached 75-120s while `api.log` showed the server answering the same requests promptly. The stack was never slow -- batch the whole smoke into one payload rather than one request per round trip. **Files added:** `docs/reports/phase5_import_provenance_smoke.console.txt`. **Files changed:** `server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py`, `tests/test_import_provenance_routes.py`, `.env.example`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3 49/72, v2 43/72, mnw 2. **Test baseline 397 -> 399.** **Flag state:** `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE=1` in `.env` for the smoke and **0** in `.env.example`, i.e. still default-OFF for anyone else;
