- 2026-07-26 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01 Phase 4 -- the minimal verification surface: the repository proved through the backend boundary**): Phase 3 put the rules a schema cannot state into a module that nothing could reach. Phase 4 is the least API that proves the module works from outside itself, and no more than that. **`server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py`** exposes 15 routes under `/api/import-provenance` -- batch create / list / get / counts / bind-trip / close / reopen / hidden, candidate create / list / get / trip / decision / hidden, and an `enums` route so a future UI reads the vocabulary from the server instead of hardcoding it -- registered in `server/code/api/main.py` and **gated default-OFF behind `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE=1`**. With the flag off every route returns **404, not 403**, because a disabled surface should not advertise that it exists; a gate test compares the router's own route table against the list it covers, so a route added later without a gate fails there rather than shipping ungated. **The router contains no database logic of its own.** It is a boundary over the Phase 3 repository, and the only SQL it runs is a read-only ownership lookup -- which is the one place I deliberately did not just forward and trust. **Boundaries are re-checked at the route layer**, per Chris's instruction: before the repository is called, a trip is resolved to its `trips.person_id` and a photo to its `photos.narrator_id`, so a trip belonging to another person, a photo belonging to another narrator, and a `person_id`/`trip_id` pair that disagree all come back **409** at the edge. That last case is an error on purpose and not an empty list: *this person has nothing on that trip* and *that trip is not theirs* are two different facts, and letting them share an answer is precisely the silent failure Phase 0 found in the existing photo clustering code. **Note for anyone reading the model later: photos are owned by `narrator_id`, not `person_id`** -- same ids, different column name, and the boundary check has to know that. **Every repository exception maps to a deliberate status.** `BatchNotFoundError` / `CandidateNotFoundError` to 404; `CrossPersonError`, `CrossTripError`, `IntakeIsNotApprovalError` and `BatchClosedError` to 409; `ExternalTokenError` and `InvalidStateError` to 400. Only the base `ImportRepositoryError` reaches 500, and that is correct, because it is raised when the database itself has drifted -- 0037 unapplied, or an approval column grown by hand -- which is a server condition and not a bad request. Ten tests assert that a rejected token returns 400 **and that the token text appears nowhere in the response body**, since a guard that refuses a credential and then echoes it back in an error message has not protected anything. **The shipped names win over the plan's names.** Chris's Phase 4 list said `review_status` and `operator_decision_json`; migration 0037 shipped `state`, `state_reason`, `reviewed_by_user_id` and `reviewed_at`. Per his own rule -- *do not keep the wrong names just because the plan said them* -- the routes use the 0037 names, and the plan's enum values `needs_review`, `changed` and `skipped`, which 0037 never shipped, are refused with **400 rather than quietly mapped onto something adjacent**, pinned by a test named for exactly that so a later author cannot make the mapping by accident. **What is deliberately absent:** no `@router.delete` anywhere in the module; no `person_id` on the candidate-create body, because a candidate copies its batch's person and offering the field would invite crossing; no route that can set `narrator_ready` or `include_in_memoir`; and no Google Photos, no Takeout, no Evidence Queue UI, no Lori behavior. **One defect found, reported plainly, and fixed where the code lives.** The list endpoint's own docstring promises *ordered oldest-first because a review queue is a queue* -- and it was not delivering that. `_now()` in `import_repository.py` formats to whole seconds, so `ORDER BY created_at ASC, id` tiebreaks on a **uuid**, which is to say at random, for every row created inside the same second -- which for a real import is the entire batch. This is a Phase 3 defect, it sits directly on the Phase 4 path (the list route is the deliverable), so under the standing autonomy rule I fixed it in the Phase 3 module rather than papering over it in the router: both `batch_list` and `candidates_list` now tiebreak on `rowid`, which is insertion order, after confirming 0037 declares no `WITHOUT ROWID`. Locked by a new `QueueOrderTests` class **in the Phase 3 suite**, not the Phase 4 one, so the test lives next to the code it constrains: `tests/test_import_repository.py` goes 50 -> 53. **Proof:** 65 new route tests green in six classes -- `FlagGateTests` 4, `BatchRouteTests` 15, `CandidateRouteTests` 16, `DecisionRouteTests` 10, `TokenRefusalRouteTests` 10, `NoDeleteAndWiringTests` 5 -- on a fixture with two people (Christopher Todd Horne and Kent James Horne) and one trip each, so every boundary test has a real second person to try to cross into. `DecisionRouteTests` includes a full before/after row comparison proving that accepting a candidate writes nothing at all to the `photos` row. Regressions re-run clean: 0037 migration lock 37, utcnow-deprecation scan 2, source-scan helpers 38, travel-doc doctrine 6, evidence lifecycle 20, import repository 53. **Two things I could not verify the obvious way, stated so the next session does not re-investigate them.** (1) `code.api.main` must be imported from `server/`, not `server/code/` -- from the latter it fails at `routers/kawa.py:18` with *attempted relative import beyond top-level package*. (2) starlette 1.0.0 / fastapi 0.139.2 keep included routers **lazy**: after 40 `include_router` calls `app.routes` holds `_IncludedRouter` objects, not flattened `APIRoute`s, so path introspection returns nothing for *any* router including `trips`, which I did not touch. That is not a wiring defect; the wiring test therefore asserts textually against `main.py`'s source, which is version-proof. **Two WO-2 inputs recorded and not acted on.** The Epic Plan's `import_candidate` lists `proposed_trip_day_id`, `proposed_trip_region_id` and `proposed_trip_stop_id`; **0037 has none of them**, so placement can only be expressed at trip granularity today, and the Evidence Review Queue will need a migration 0038 to say more. And the plan's `changed` (accepted at a different placement than proposed) and `skipped` (softer than rejected) have no 0037 state at all. Both are WO-2 design inputs, not Phase 4 blockers, which is why **Phase 4 stayed no-migration**. **Files added:** `server/code/api/routers/import_provenance.py`, `tests/test_import_provenance_routes.py`. **Files changed:** `server/code/api/main.py`, `server/code/api/services/import_repository.py`, `tests/test_import_repository.py`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3 49/72, v2 43/72, mnw 2. **Test baseline 329 -> 397.** **Flag state:** `HORNELORE_IMPORT_PROVENANCE` is **new** and **default OFF**; every other flag unchanged, and no flag was read or written at import time. **No migration, no UI and no live-database write in this phase.** **Open items:** (1) Phase 5, the live smoke -- disposable trip, fake batch, fake candidates, list, accept one, skip one, hide one, and confirm nothing became narrator-facing or memoir-approved, which needs the flag on a running stack; (2) the two WO-2 inputs above -- the missing `proposed_*` columns and the homeless `changed`/`skipped` values; (3) `WO-E2E-HARNESS-SANDBOX-01` stays recorded and unworked, and the e2e suite stays parked; (4) `audit_identity_preflight.py` still exits 1 on the five forbidden harness clusters and should stay that way; (5) still parked -- the `trip_bio_suggestions` force-delete decision. **Scope wall unchanged:** no identity architecture, no merge tooling, no canonical pointer, no general duplicate cleanup, no Walt/Test/Kent harness cleanup; and no Google Photos, Takeout, Lori review, export or bio_suggestions work until the Import Provenance landing zone is finished.
- 2026-07-26 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01 Phase 3 -- the import repository: the rules a schema cannot state, made procedural**): Migration 0037 stated four rules in the only language a schema has, which is the presence and absence of columns and constraints -- and three of them SQLite cannot state at all. `import_batch` carries `person_id` and `trip_id` as **independent** foreign keys, so nothing in the database stops a batch owned by one person from pointing at another person's trip; that is precisely the class of confusion the two Christopher rows produced, and it is worth being blunt that the schema alone would have let it happen again. `external_ref` is plain `TEXT`, so nothing stops an OAuth access token from landing in it. And the absence of `narrator_ready` prevents that column from being set but cannot stop a future import path from reaching sideways into `photos` and setting it there. **`server/code/api/services/import_repository.py`** is where those three become procedure. **Path note, because the earlier docs were wrong:** there is no `services/` directory at the repo root; the import repository lives beside `trip_repository.py` in `server/code/api/services/`, and the spec and checklist both said otherwise. **Second doc correction:** 0037's column is `match_reason_json`, singular, not `match_reasons_json`. **Intake is not approval.** `candidate_create` takes no `state` parameter and no approval flag of any kind -- asserted by a test that reads the function's own `inspect.signature` -- so every candidate is born `pending`. `candidate_decide` is the only exit, and `'accepted'` **requires** a `photo_id` that already exists and is owned by the candidate's person: the repository never creates a photos row, so accepting records a decision about a photo the operator already has rather than manufacturing one. `rejected` / `duplicate` / `error` all **refuse** a `photo_id`; `'pending'` is not a decision; an unknown state is refused. A test accepts a candidate and then asserts `photos.narrator_ready`, `needs_confirmation`, `date_approved_for_lori` and `location_approved_for_lori` are all untouched. And because the absence of a column is only enforcement while it stays absent, every write runs `PRAGMA table_info(import_candidate)` and raises if `narrator_ready` or `include_in_memoir` has appeared -- proved by a test that does the `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` and watches the guard fire. **The person/trip boundary.** A candidate does not accept a `person_id`; it copies its batch's. A batch cannot be created on, or bound to, a trip belonging to someone else. A candidate cannot claim a trip its person does not own, cannot disagree with the trip its batch is bound to, and inherits that trip when the batch has one. Accepting refuses a photo belonging to another person. Person-filtered lists never leak. **No raw external tokens.** Every operator-supplied string is scanned before it is written -- Google OAuth access (`ya29.`) and refresh (`1//`) tokens, GitHub `ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghu_`/`ghs_`/`ghr_`, OpenAI `sk-`, JWTs, `Bearer` headers, `authorization:` lines, and credential-bearing query strings -- plus a key-name check over `match_reason` that recurses to a bounded depth of 12. The guard is calibrated rather than merely loud: a test proves **real Google Photos media ids and real Takeout archive filenames still pass**, which is what keeps a future author from disabling it the first time it blocks legitimate data. **Reversible.** The module contains no `DELETE FROM` and no `DROP TABLE` -- a test reads the module's own source and asserts both strings are absent. Hide is a flag, hide/unhide preserves match reasons and state and state reason, and hidden rows still count toward batch totals. Counters are **recomputed** from the candidate rows on every state change rather than incremented, so a replayed decision cannot drift them. `candidate_create` honours 0037's UNIQUE `(batch_id, external_id)` by returning the existing id instead of raising, so re-running the same fetch adds nothing and the first write wins; the same external id in a different batch is correctly a new row. **Proof:** 50 new tests, all green, in six classes -- `IntakeIsNotApprovalTests` 9, `BoundaryTests` 11, `NoTokenTests` 9, `MatchReasonRoundTripTests` 5, `ReversibilityTests` 4, `BatchLifecycleTests` 12 -- on a fixture that builds two people and one trip each, so every boundary test has a real second person to try to cross into. One of those tests establishes something the code did **not** need to change for: a person hard-delete cascades `import_batch` and `import_candidate` through 0037's foreign keys, so neither table needs adding to `hard_delete_person`'s explicit 14-table list. Regressions re-run clean: the 0037 migration lock 37, the utcnow-deprecation scan 2 (the new module uses `datetime.now(timezone.utc)`), source-scan helpers 38, travel-doc doctrine 6, evidence lifecycle 20. **One thing I did not do, stated plainly:** the parked Playwright e2e suite is in worse shape than "not re-run since the pin" suggests -- `lorevox-smoke-flow.spec.ts` POSTs a person, a profile and an interview session into the **live** database with no `afterAll` and no cleanup at all; `test_narrator_switch.spec.ts` creates three people and cleans up only if `afterAll` runs; `lori_behavior_surfaces.spec.js` points at `/hornelore1.0.html` when the real path is `/ui/hornelore1.0.html` and looks for a narrator who is not in this database; `lorevox-ui-audit.spec.ts` targets `ui/lori9.0.html` on port 8080, a file that does not exist and a port nothing listens on; and `package.json` has six scripts pointing at four spec files that do not exist. Repairing and running it would have written harness residue into the database Chris had just carefully reset -- the exact class of residue that produces the Walt / Trip Canary / Kent clusters he explicitly forbade cleaning up -- and it is a different subsystem from the one this phase names, so I left it alone and reported it instead. **Files added:** `server/code/api/services/import_repository.py`, `tests/test_import_repository.py`. **Files changed:** `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3 49/72, v2 43/72, mnw 2. **Test baseline 279 -> 329.** **Flag state:** unchanged; no flag was read or written. **No API route, no UI, no migration and no live-database write in this phase** -- the module is not yet wired to a route, which is Phase 4. **Open items:** (1) Phase 4, the minimal verification surface -- operator-only list/create/decide, the least UI that lets the Phase 5 smoke happen, and explicitly not the Evidence Review Queue; (2) the e2e suite above stays parked and is Chris's call, not an agent's; (3) `audit_identity_preflight.py` still exits 1 on the five forbidden harness clusters and should stay that way; (4) still parked -- the `trip_bio_suggestions` force-delete decision. **Scope wall unchanged:** no identity architecture, no merge tooling, no canonical pointer, no general duplicate cleanup, no Walt/Test/Kent harness cleanup; and no Google Photos, Takeout, Lori review, export or bio_suggestions work until the Import Provenance landing zone is finished.

REFERENCES photos(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (6 indexes, UNIQUE `(trip_id, photo_id)` preserved), closing F2 and F4. Then `import_batch` (18 cols, 3 indexes) and `import_candidate` (25 cols, 6 indexes, UNIQUE `(batch_id, external_id)`), both with `0036`'s reversible `hidden`/`hidden_at`. `import_candidate.photo_id` is **SET NULL, not CASCADE** -- losing a photo must not silently destroy the record of how it arrived. **`import_candidate` has no `narrator_ready` column, no `include_in_memoir` column and no token or credential columns of any kind; the absence is the enforcement of *import is intake, not approval*, because a column that does not exist cannot be set by a future import path that forgets the rule.** `tests/test_import_provenance_foundation_migration.py` asserts that directly, including that setting `state='accepted'` flips no photo flag. **Proof:** 37 new tests all green; applied through the real `run_pending_migrations` against two copies of the live database (pre-wipe and post-wipe) with zero row loss across all seven photo/link tables, both FKs present with CASCADE, `foreign_key_check` empty, `integrity_check` ok, 20 indexes, and a clean second-run no-op; regression across the 20 affected suites **539 tests, OK (skipped=6)**. - 2026-07-26 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01 Phase 0 + Phase 1 -- the epic's foundation was surveyed before anything was built, and the survey found an identity fault line under it**): Recon, one read-only script and docs. **No schema, no migration, no API, no test, no flag, no UI -- not one line of application code.** The stack stayed down; nothing here needs a smoke. Chris's gate was *"pushed. go to work"* on a front door that was finally clean, so this is work order 1 of 8 from the Epic Plan, opened at Phase 0 rather than at the migration. **What the survey found.** `grep` for `import_batch`/`import_candidate` returns nothing anywhere -- the foundation is greenfield and the next migration number is 0037. But the Epic Plan's `import_candidate` carries **both** `person_id` and `photo_id`, and those two columns live in namespaces the schema never forces into agreement: `photos.narrator_id` (`0001_lori_photo_shared.sql:15`) is `TEXT NOT NULL` with **no FOREIGN KEY**, while `trips.person_id` (`0034_trips_person_id_fk.sql`) **REFERENCES people(id)**. The application equates them by convention in six places (`routers/trips.py:500` and `:702`, `routers/chat_ws.py:933` and `:1839`, `routers/people.py:190` and `:577`), the load-bearing one being `narrator_id = req.narrator_id or trip.get("person_id")` handed straight to `_photos_for_narrator()`, which filters `photos WHERE narrator_id = ?` and returns an **empty list, not an error**, when the two disagree -- so photo clustering against a mismatched id reports a successful run with zero matches. `trip_photo_links.photo_id` (`0015_trip_tables.sql:90`) has no `REFERENCES` either, and its unique index is `(trip_id, photo_id)` -- scoped by trip, never by owner. `people` is flat, with no alias column, no canonical pointer, and **no person-merge tooling anywhere in the repository**. **The live database turned out to be reachable, which changes the session's outcome.** Every prior session recorded it as unreachable, and that was true then; `C:\hornelore_data` is now a connected folder alongside the repo, so `hornelore.sqlite3` was copied into the container (md5 `fddf635a7526aa960495deb30d1ed65c`, verified against the device both before and after), opened `mode=ro`, and audited there. **The live file was never opened by the agent and nothing was written to `C:\hornelore_data`.** **Verdict: IDENTITY SPLIT DETECTED, exit 1.** Both Christopher rows carry real data -- `a4b2f07a` (Christopher Todd Horne) holds 13 photos, 6 photo_sessions, 3 story_candidates and **both trips**; `e7fdb578` (Christopher) holds 6 photos and 9 bio_facts. **And it is widening the wrong way:** every photo on `a4b2f07a` predates 2026-07-13, while all six on `e7fdb578` were uploaded 2026-07-25 between 03:33 and 23:31 -- the Phase 5/6 smoke residue of backlog item 5 -- so new photo activity is landing on the id that owns **zero** trips. The crossing-link and empty-cluster queries are nonetheless **clean** (0 links with a missing photo, 0 links crossing owners, 0 of 2 trips clustering against an empty set): the predicted failure has not fired, and has not fired only because both trips and all 13 linked photos happen to sit on the same id. One trip created under `e7fdb578` fires it. **Every orphan count is zero** (`photos -> people`, `trip_photo_links -> photos`, and `photo_sessions`/`bio_facts`/`story_candidates -> people`), so the FK-adding rebuild is measured-safe against today's rows -- the 0034-class failure that made it expensive is not in this data. Five of the six flagged clusters are harness residue (Walt x7, Trip Canary x5 and x4, Esteban x3, Kent x3), which matters twice: `people` is 37 rows of which roughly two thirds are test rows, and **nothing in the data distinguishes a harness duplicate from a real split**. **The honest limit, stated so it is not misread later:** a foreign key stops a reference pointing at *nothing*; it does not stop one pointing at *the wrong one of two valid rows*. Adding the FKs would leave the Christopher question completely untouched. That question is promoted to its own phase and blocks the first import, not the migration. **Files changed:** `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Files added:** `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-IMPORT-PROVENANCE-FOUNDATION-01_Spec.md`, `scripts/audit_identity_preflight.py`, `docs/reports/identity_preflight_20260726.console.txt`. **Flag state: unchanged.** **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2 -- no extractor-lane code touched, nothing executable added to any import path. **Test baseline unchanged at 279** -- no test file was touched and none was run; the one new script is operator-invoked and read-only. **Open items:** two decisions belong to Chris and both block Phase 2 -- (1) the two Christopher rows: merge onto `a4b2f07a`, keep them deliberately separate, or add a `merged_into` canonical pointer (the reversible shape, and the one recommended if a merge is wanted); (2) A/B/C for `import_candidate`'s identity key, with B -- add the missing FKs -- now measured safe and recommended. Carried and unchanged: `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` still not re-run since the Playwright 1.58.2 pin (needs the stack up), the `bio_suggestions` force-delete-from-birth backend/product call, and the rest of the eight-item post-unification backlog. **A caution worth recording for the next session:** `device_stage_files` silently returned a **stale** copy of `CLAUDE.md` and `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md` from a previous session's staging -- `ok:true` with the correct byte count in the response, 60KB short on disk, and the docs-reconcile commit absent from it. Editing that copy and writing it back would have silently reverted the front door. Deleting the stale staged files and re-staging fixed it. **md5-verify every staged file against the device before editing it**, exactly as the mount-corruption guard already requires after an Edit. A phase is a scope wall.

- 2026-07-26 (**WO-DOC-RECONCILE-01 LANDED -- documentation reconciliation after the unification arc**): Chris asked for a review of the code, the work and the documents before starting the epic, and to finish what needed finishing. The phase arc itself needed nothing -- all six phases carry 'what landed' sections with their own verification, Phase 6 carries the close-out, and the two deviations from the Phase 0 plan (the Documenter not deleted, the test suites not folded) were ordered, argued and carried as backlog rather than skipped. **The documents were the gap.** `README.md`, the front door, still described 2026-07-24: no mention of `WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01` anywhere in the file including the Work Orders table, 'Travel Doc Lab depth' named as the active lane, and a MEDIUM known issue -- native `window.prompt()` in the evidence panel -- that was already fixed; verified at zero executable `prompt`/`confirm`/`alert` in `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js` with all six textual occurrences being comments, and pinned by the surface gates. A neighbouring entry, the `travel-documenter.js` double-send, was true but mis-scoped: that module is retired and off the operator path since Phase 4, which the entry did not say. Both corrected in place rather than deleted -- fixed and moved-off-path are different facts and a reader needs to be able to tell them apart. In the spec, Phases 4/5/6 had full landed sections but no marker in the Phases index, so a skim showed three open phases; and Phase 6 Finding 1 is now marked resolved by WO-HARNESS-DEPS-01. **Post-unification backlog went from seven items to eight**: item 5 (smoke-photo residue) is taken as documented test rows because the live DB is outside the repo at `/mnt/c/hornelore_data/db/hornelore.sqlite3` and unreachable from either filesystem an agent has, and new item 8 promotes Phase 6 Finding 4 -- two person records reading as one narrator -- because the epic writes provenance against `person_id` and would split an import across them silently. **One decision left with Chris, deliberately not taken:** whether `bio_suggestions` should count toward `requires_force`. `_TRIP_DEPENDENT_TABLES` already excludes `trip_themes` on the stated grounds that the gate keys on evidence lanes and lightweight auto-written labels must not break empty-trip semantics; `trip_bio_suggestions` is written by `trip_timeline_bridge.sync_trip_to_life_record` on every create, one row, `status='suggested'`, derived from the trip rather than contributed to it, and so fails that same test on every count -- but it is a backend change with a test consequence (the ten-lane pin) and belongs to him, not to an agent mid-docs-pass. Files changed: `README.md`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. Files added: none. Active baseline unchanged: `r5h-followup-guard-v1` (78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2). Flag state unchanged. Test baseline unchanged and re-verified this session at **279 green** (221 + 58 across the eight suites). Open items: the eight-item post-unification backlog; the `bio_suggestions` decision above; the `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` audit suite still has not been re-run since the 1.58.2 pin; the new epic remains a separate session.

- 2026-07-26 (**WO-HARNESS-DEPS-01 LANDED -- the Node Playwright toolchain is declared, pinned and documented; both liveness harnesses now run on Chris's machine**): Config and docs only. No `ui/`, no `api/`, no `server/`, no schema, no flag, no test file changed, and no test count moved. Closes the most actionable of the five Phase 6 findings. **The diagnosis was not a missing dependency.** `package.json` has declared `playwright` and `playwright-core` the whole time and is byte-identical device-to-container (`94dbd8a2`); `.gitignore` line 61 excludes `node_modules/`, correctly, which means the packages were never going to arrive with `git pull` and nothing in git could restore them once lost. **The browsers were already on disk and matched exactly.** `~/.cache/ms-playwright` holds `chromium-1208 chromium_headless_shell-1208 ffmpeg-1011 firefox-1509 webkit-2248`; pulling `browsers.json` out of each candidate release maps that five-for-five onto Playwright **1.58.2**, the exact version the manifest names. So the install was done properly once and only the half that lives inside the repo was lost -- which also explains why the diagnostic showed no Python Playwright at all: the toolchain `scripts/ui/README.md` documents is the one that is *not* installed. **The caret was the active hazard.** `^1.58.2` resolves to 1.62.0 today, which wants `chromium-1234`, so the obvious `npm install` would have downloaded a second ~500 MB browser set and stranded the one already there; the same trap sat on `@playwright/test: ^1.54.2`. All three Playwright entries are now pinned exact. Tailwind's two carets are deliberately left alone as out of scope -- `package-lock.json` holds the rest of the tree still and is committed for exactly that reason, since without a lockfile a *reproducibility* harness was resolving a different browser build on every install (manifest 1.58.2, container proof 1.56.0, a fresh install today 1.62.0 -- three versions across three moments). **The root cause was documentation, not configuration.** The string `npm install` appeared in zero `.md` files in this repo and `node scripts/ui/` in zero; `scripts/ui/README.md` documented one of that folder's five harnesses and never mentioned either `.js` file, so the setup step for half the folder's contents had simply never been written down. That README now opens by naming both toolchains and stating that neither arrives with `git pull`, splits Setup into Python and Node halves, explains the exact-pin rationale with the concrete revision mapping, documents the `NODE_PATH` and `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_PATH` overrides both harnesses already honoured, gives the WSL home-directory install that avoids writing `node_modules/` across the `/mnt/c` 9p boundary, and adds a full section for each liveness harness including the parked-fetch scenario table, why `control_live` is the load-bearing row, why the census counts bind/unbind rather than observing effects, and why a green Python suite is not a substitute for running them. Also narrowed the stale Phase 4 plan line in `WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, which still read as though the retired Documenter and the dev harness were slated for deletion, with a pointer to the "What deliberately did NOT come out" section that already explained the divergence; raised in review, docs-only, no bearing on code. Files changed: `package.json`, `scripts/ui/README.md`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. Files added: `package-lock.json` (generated by `npm install` on the device and committed alongside). Active baseline unchanged: `r5h-followup-guard-v1` (78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2). Flag state unchanged. Open items: the six remaining post-unification backlog entries at the foot of the spec are untouched (`travel-doc-lab.js` rename, retired Documenter deletion, the `bio_suggestions` force-delete-from-birth backend/product question, selection persistence across a tab round-trip, smoke-photo residue at 6 on narrator `e7fdb578`, and a live smoke for WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01); the `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` audit suite shares this manifest and now resolves against 1.58.2 too, which is a change in what it runs against and has not been re-run; and the new epic (Import Provenance Foundation through Export Pipeline) remains a separate session.

- 2026-07-25 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 6 LANDED -- WO CLOSED; the unified Travel Doc is proved on the live operator path**): Live verification and docs only. **Not one line of code was written** -- no `ui/`, no `api/`, no `server/`, no schema, no flag, no test file changed. **Thirty-seven steps, thirty-seven green, zero defects.** Ref-based and coordinate-based clicking were abandoned in the first few steps because refs churn on every re-render inside the workspace; interaction ran instead through a page-side helper that resolves each element by text and structure at call time, and every persistence claim was read back from `/api/trips/{id}/tree` server-side rather than off the DOM. Dialogs, listeners, sockets, console errors and non-2xx traffic came from an instrumentation spy installed before the mount rather than from inspection -- including a `WebSocket.prototype.send` wrap, so the Lori modal scope was proved from the **actual outbound `start_turn` frame** carrying `surface: "travel_doc_modal"` and the trip's own session id, not from the label on screen. **Surface:** the Travel Doc tab mounts the unified workspace directly, with no fallback toggle, no `#lvTravelDocHost`, no retired Documenter UI, and zero rendered occurrences of *legacy*, *production Travel Doc*, *UI Lab*, *experimental*, *lab-only*, *removable* or *documenter*. **Structure:** a disposable trip with dates generated its day cards; two regions and nine stops built including a three-deep substop group; edits at all four levels; regions and stops reordered; substop arrows confined to their sibling group; a cross-region move through the edit drawer; insert-before and insert-after on both a top-level stop and a substop; order surviving a full refresh, verified server-side. **Intake:** photo and source upload both green, with the `FileList` node identity held across a scope retarget -- injecting a real `DataTransfer`-backed `File` into the drawer's own visible input turned out to be a stronger test of that invariant than a native picker would have been, because it made the retarget observable. Cluster stated its whole-library caveat in-panel. **The lifecycle census across three leave/re-enter cycles is the load-bearing proof:** read as deltas, every entry opened exactly what the previous exit closed -- never more than one live BroadcastChannel, never more than one live document keydown listener, and the Lori socket opened exactly once in the entire run and closed cleanly on the first leave. Zero native dialogs, zero console errors, zero page errors, zero unhandled rejections. **The force-delete gate rendered all ten impact lanes and `bio_suggestions` was HOT at 1** -- the Phase 3A finding reproducing live rather than in theory, because production's nine-lane grid would have shown that evidence nowhere while the backend refused the delete. Arming was tested against a fifteen-case matrix, not a happy path: case folding, prefix, suffix, one character short and one character long all refused; only the exact title, the exact id, or those with surrounding whitespace armed. The forced delete completed and the narrator's trip list came back empty from the API. **Quarantine holds:** the dev harness still mounts and loads a real workspace; the retired Documenter is still served (122262 bytes) and is unreachable from the shell -- across 82 script tags no travel-documenter asset is requested, no link points at it, its globals are `undefined`, and all six remaining textual references across `app.js` and `travel-doc-lab.js` are inside comments. 279 tests green; both Playwright liveness harnesses PASS (14/14 and 23/23). **Five findings, none a defect.** The most actionable: **the two liveness harnesses cannot run on Chris's machine at all** -- `node_modules` does not exist in the working tree, so they fail with `playwright is not installed`; they were run in the cloud container after `md5sum` proved the eight relevant files byte-identical, which is sound but means part of the proof chain is not reproducible where the code actually lives. Also: force-delete arming trims whitespace by design and cannot loosen the server check, because the wire always carries `confirm_trip_id = the trip id` no matter what was typed; the smoke photo survived the cascade (photo *links* are deleted, photos are not), putting narrator `e7fdb578`'s library at **six** smoke photos, up from five after Phase 4; `e7fdb578` and `a4b2f07a` are two distinct person records that both read as Christopher, which the harness picker and the shell header can make look like one narrator; and cluster wrote links for six photos, five of them pre-existing library photos, exactly as its own caveat states. **WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 is CLOSED** -- one Travel Doc on the operator path, no toggle, no second implementation reachable, no native dialog, no leaked listener or socket, and the destructive path gated exactly as `WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01` requires. Seven-item post-unification backlog recorded at the foot of the spec: the `travel-doc-lab.js` rename, deleting the retired standalone Documenter (which is also what would make the Phase 5 test fold mechanical), the `bio_suggestions` force-delete-from-birth backend/product question, selection persistence across a tab round-trip, smoke-photo residue, a live smoke for `WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01` itself, and the post-unification epic. **Files changed:** `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`. **Files added:** none. **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` (78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2) -- no extractor-lane code exists in this phase, and no code exists in it at all. **Flag state:** unchanged; no env flag introduced, read or modified. **Open items:** the seven-item post-unification backlog above; the harness/`node_modules` gap is the one worth deciding first, because it silently narrows what Chris can verify himself.

- 2026-07-25 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 5 LANDED -- one Travel Doc test surface; the Lab-vs-Documenter seam is gone from the suites too**): Tests and docs only; **no production file touched at all** -- no `ui/`, no `server/`, no backend, no API, no schema, no flag change, and nothing deleted. **Two files added.** `tests/travel_doc_surfaces.py` is the single answer to "what are the Travel Doc surfaces, and which of them is on the operator path": eleven `Surface` records, each carrying a path, a prose ROLE and an `on_operator_path` flag, plus the comment stripper and the two shell-region extractors. `tests/test_travel_doc_surface_gates.py` holds the cross-cutting doctrine that until now was asserted once per originating work order and therefore only on the file that work order happened to touch -- no native dialog on any Travel Doc region, the retired module stays quarantined, the Phase 4 inversion proved in one place, evidence lanes hide-only, and exactly one mount caller off the shell. **Six suites retargeted:** between them `test_travel_doc_lab.py`, `test_travel_doc_shell_mount.py`, `test_travel_documenter_panel.py`, `test_trip_lane_fixpack_js.py`, `test_travel_doc_evidence_ui.py` and `test_travel_doc_livetest_fixes.py` carried thirty private `Path` literals and eleven private copies of the stripper and the extractors, so moving a file meant six edits and the sixth was the one always missed. They are thin aliases to the map now, and **not one test body moved**, so no coverage shifted. **The premise the order was written on turned out to be half true, which is worth saying plainly:** Phase 4 had already narrowed most of the assertions Phase 5 expected to find stale, and had left a post-Phase-4 docstring on each explaining the inversion. Exactly one thing was actually stale and it was a *name*: `test_lab_does_not_import_production_module` still asserted something true and still worth asserting, while its name described a world in which the Documenter was production and this module was an experiment beside it. Renamed `test_the_unified_module_never_loads_the_retired_one`; assertion untouched. One real duplicate was narrowed -- `test_force_delete_gate_needs_no_fallback_surface` and `test_the_fallback_deep_link_is_gone_module_wide` both made the same module-wide claim about `prodTravelDocUrl`, so a future narrowing of that escape hatch had two owners and no clear one; the module-wide claim now has exactly one. **A wart made visible rather than fixed or hidden:** the retired `travel-documenter.js` still carries exactly two native confirm calls (region delete, stop delete) and still pre-stringifies its request bodies. Repairing it is work on a module nothing on the operator path loads and is out of this phase; deleting the assertion would have hidden it. The count is pinned at exactly two, so a third fails the build, and a separate gate fails the build if that module is ever re-attached to the operator path. **A surface nobody had inventoried:** `scripts/ui/run_travel_doc_mount_liveness.js`, the Phase 1.1 proof, appeared in no map and in no doctrine scan. It is in both now, and both liveness harnesses are scanned for dialogs, because a harness that opens one teaches the next reader that dialogs are acceptable here and a liveness run that hits one hangs instead of failing. **The mutation check found a live hole in a gate written minutes earlier:** `assertIn("lvTravelDocMount", block)` also matches the `typeof window.lvTravelDocMount === "function"` guard on the line above the call, so that gate would have stayed green against a shell that checked whether it could mount and then never did. It now pins the call and the handle assignment, because the handle is what teardown later has to find. Twelve mutations, twelve killed, each injected into an in-memory copy of a surface so nothing on disk moved. **Where this deviates from the written plan, deliberately:** the Phase 0 spec said fold both suites into one `tests/test_travel_doc.py`. That is not what landed. The two files guard two different modules that both still exist and are both still served; concatenating 129 tests and 36 tests would produce a 125K module whose name claims a boundary it cannot hold, and would move every line number in both, burying the diff a reviewer needs to see. Chris's order asked for "one coherent Travel Doc test surface", which is a coherence requirement, and coherence came from a shared map plus one doctrine file while each suite kept its own subject. If Chris wants the literal fold anyway it is mechanical, and it should be its own commit. **Files changed:** `tests/test_travel_doc_lab.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_shell_mount.py`, `tests/test_travel_documenter_panel.py`, `tests/test_trip_lane_fixpack_js.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_evidence_ui.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_livetest_fixes.py`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Files added:** `tests/travel_doc_surfaces.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_surface_gates.py`. **Files removed:** none. **Flag state: unchanged.** **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2 -- no extractor-lane code touched, and no product code touched at all. **Tests: 279 green (was 262)** across the six suites plus the two new files in 7.6s, with the eight adjacent Travel Doc suites re-run green as well (208 tests, 6 pre-existing skips). **Open items:** the `travel-doc-lab.js` -> `travel-doc.js` rename stays parked; the dev harness page still calls itself both "Travel Doc UI Lab" in rendered copy and `DEV HARNESS` in its own header, still a parked copy edit; the retired module's two native confirm calls are pinned, not repaired; Phase 6 live smoke stays OPEN. Backlog carried, still not gates: the bio-suggestion-blocks-every-delete backend question, selection not surviving a tab round-trip, narrator `e7fdb578`'s five disposable smoke photos, and the 2026-07-23 live smoke of `WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01`. A phase is a scope wall.)

- 2026-07-25 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 4 LANDED and smoke-accepted -- the split is gone from the operator path; there is one Travel Doc, not two behind a toggle**): Removal phase, front-end only; **no backend, no API, no schema, no flag change**, and **no endpoint deleted** -- Chris's requirement 7. **What came out of the shell:** the surface toggle and everything that served it -- `_LV_TD_SURFACE_KEY`, `_lvTravelDocSurface()`, `_lvTravelDocDestroyLegacy()`, `_lvTravelDocPaintSurfaceChrome()` and `window.lvTravelDocSetSurface` from `ui/js/app.js`; the two legacy asset tags, the switch row and the `#lvTravelDocHost` div from `ui/hornelore1.0.html`; the `.lv-td-surface-*`, `.lv-td-host-off` and three `body.lv-td-focus` rules from `ui/css/lori80.css`; and `prodTravelDocUrl()` plus the `.tdl-route-legacy` foot-note that deep-linked out of the route board. The `traveldoc` arm of `lvShellShowTab` is now a single-host mount and `lvTravelDocTeardownAll()` a single destroy. **Nothing was deleted that a backend still serves:** the old module, its stylesheet and its standalone page all stay on disk, that page still mounts the module, and a new `LegacyModuleStillExistsTest` fails the build if any of them goes missing -- unmounting a surface and deleting a module are different acts, and only the first was ordered. **The dev harness was kept and labelled rather than deleted** (requirement 6), for three reasons now written into the page itself: `ui/travel-doc-lab.html` is the only caller of `window.lvTravelDocMount()` outside the shell and the only one that mounts *without* the shell's identity flag -- the flag that governs branding, layout and whether the querystring may supply a narrator id, so without this page that entire branch would have no caller and would rot untested; it mounts against a bare host with no shell chrome, which is how a layout or teardown bug the shell happens to mask shows up early; and it parks the handle on `window` so a developer can drive teardown from the console. It now declares `DEV HARNESS -- DEV-ONLY, REMOVABLE` at the top and names exactly what to delete if it ever goes -- and, more usefully, what NOT to delete with it. **Five things Phase 4 fixed that the work order did not name, all squarely on the removal path.** (1) `lv80SwitchPerson`'s teardown fallback nulled `window._lvTravelDocMountedFor`, the retired Documenter's marker. That marker no longer exists anywhere, so the fallback had quietly become a no-op that would have left a stale mount alive on any load where `app.js` failed; it nulls `_lvTravelDocUnifiedMountedFor` now, and a test pins it. (2) The day photo picker's empty state was **ungated and operator-visible** and read *"add them via Photo Intake, then cluster from the production Travel Doc"* -- a dead end twice over, since there is no production Travel Doc left to reach and Phase 3C put both Upload and Cluster on this surface's own toolbar; it now names the buttons that actually do the job. (3) `renderEvalChecklist`'s header comment and its rendered copy described this module as *"part of the removable lab, not production Travel Doc"*; it carries dev-harness framing now, and the test guarding it also pins the `!embedded` call site so the copy and the gate cannot drift apart. (4) The route board's deep-link foot-note and its CSS rule came out together. (5) The three `body.lv-td-focus` rules in `lori80.css` had become unreachable the moment the Documenter left the shell path. **A gate that looked obvious and was wrong.** The acceptance list asks for no visible "legacy" on the normal shell path, and a file-wide `assertNotIn("legacy")` over `hornelore1.0.html` looks like the way to prove it -- but the shell carries **19** unrelated occurrences (the QF live-ownership override, the retired `/api/facts/add` path, `LorevoxEras.legacyKeyToEraId`, welcome-back fallback prose). That gate would have been a permanent false alarm about somebody else's code, so it is scoped to the Travel Doc panel plus the `app.js` mount block, with the reason written into the test. The strongest gate is a different one: `hornelore1.0.html` now contains **zero raw occurrences** of the old module's name, comments included, so `test_the_shell_never_names_the_legacy_module` can assert against the un-stripped file -- which is why every replacement comment in the shell was deliberately written without that word. **Tests were rewritten, not deleted** (requirement 8). Five whose boundary genuinely inverted came out of `tests/test_travel_doc_shell_mount.py` -- the second-handle store, the both-destroyers pin, the destroy-the-other-surface pin, the switch-tears-both-down pin and the hiding-is-never-the-only-teardown pin -- and thirteen new ones went in, including a `LegacyFallbackIsGoneTest` that asserts each removed thing is **absent** rather than merely no longer asserted. That distinction is the whole point: an absence that is asserted fails the build when someone re-adds a `<script>` tag; an absence that is only unmentioned does not. Seven inverted tests in the other two suites were narrowed rather than dropped -- each keeps the half that still proves something (identity comes from the shell and not a paste, the tab is still wired, the tab-id migration still strands nobody, the force-delete gate is self-contained) and flips only the half Phase 4 retired. `test_lab_files_carry_removable_marker` became `test_the_harness_page_is_the_removable_one`: the old form required a delete-me sign on the two files that are now the operator's Travel Doc, so it now pins the true split and **fails if `travel-doc-lab.js` or its stylesheet ever claims to be removable again**. **Verification.** `node --check` clean on all three JS files, `ast.parse` clean on all three test files, `CR: 0` on all ten files on both sides of the transfer, **262 tests green** (was 255) across the same six suites, in the container and on the device. The load-bearing new gates were **mutation-tested rather than trusted**: eight mutants -- re-add the legacy script tag, re-add the surface toggle markup, revert the switch fallback to the retired marker, re-add the removable marker to the module, re-add the deep-link helper, strip `DEV-ONLY` from the harness page, re-add the off-host CSS rule, re-add the legacy mount call to `app.js` -- and every one was killed by its intended gate. `scripts/ui/run_travel_doc_shell_mount_liveness.js` was rewritten for one surface with a new `singleSurface` probe that reads the live document for a second host, a toggle, a surface setter or resolver, a legacy asset and a stored surface preference. Its negative controls were **kept honest rather than quietly retired**: controls 1 and 5 are still re-runnable, controls 2, 3 and 4 are explicitly marked NOT re-runnable after Phase 4, and a re-runnable substitute is named in their place (delete `_lvTravelDocDestroyUnified()` from the remount arm and confirm `narrator_switch` reports 2/2). A harness that keeps a control it can no longer run is a harness that lies about its own coverage. **Files changed:** `ui/hornelore1.0.html`, `ui/js/app.js`, `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js`, `ui/css/lori80.css`, `ui/css/travel-doc-lab.css`, `ui/travel-doc-lab.html`, `scripts/ui/run_travel_doc_shell_mount_liveness.js`, `tests/test_travel_doc_shell_mount.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_lab.py`, `tests/test_travel_documenter_panel.py`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Files added:** none. **Files removed:** none -- deliberately, per requirement 7. **Flag state: unchanged.** **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2 -- no extractor-lane code touched. **Open items:** none blocking -- **live smoke green on all fifteen of Chris's steps.** One surface and no fallback toggle in the shell (0 `[data-td-surface]`, 0 `.lv-td-surface-switch`, no `#lvTravelDocHost`, `lvTravelDocSetSurface` undefined), zero requests for the retired module across 190 resources, and zero visible occurrences of *legacy*, *production Travel Doc*, *UI Lab*, *experimental*, *lab-only*, *removable* or *documenter* anywhere in the shell's rendered text. Region and stop create, edit and reorder verified server-side through `/api/trips/{id}/tree` rather than off the DOM, with a region's stops travelling with it and no reparenting. Photo and source upload both green with `FileList` node identity held across the drawer; cluster correct including its whole-library caveat. Lori opened at `surface: travel_doc_modal` scoped to the trip. **The mount census across three leave/re-enter round trips is the load-bearing one for this phase:** instrumenting `BroadcastChannel`, `WebSocket` and `document` keydown showed each entry opening exactly what the previous exit closed -- opens 3 / closes 2, keydown +3 / -3, sockets 0 net, `.tdl-root` pinned at 1 at every sample -- so retiring the second surface did not leave a second listener behind. Force delete rendered all ten impact lanes, refused a title one character short, armed only on the exact title, and completed. Zero native dialogs across the whole run, recorded by a spy rather than by inspection, and zero console errors across a full reload plus a sweep of all eight Travel Doc tabs. The standalone harness still mounts. One observation, not a defect: the dev harness page's *rendered* copy still says "Travel Doc UI Lab" and "This experimental lab needs a narrator" and still paints the `UI Lab - experimental` badge, while the page's own HTML header declares it `DEV HARNESS -- DEV-ONLY, REMOVABLE`. All three strings sit behind `embedded ? ... : ...` ternaries (`travel-doc-lab.js` lines 1096, 1100, 1591), so they can only ever render on the standalone page and never in the shell -- which is why the shell-path copy gate came back clean. Requirement 5 is met by quarantine rather than removal, exactly as written. The harness now calls itself two different things; reconciling that is a copy edit, parked rather than taken unilaterally after the code had already been committed. Phase 5 (test consolidation into one Travel Doc suite) and Phase 6 (full live smoke) stay OPEN and explicitly out of scope here, as does the `travel-doc-lab.js` -> `travel-doc.js` rename, parked as churn that would bury a real diff. Backlog carried, explicitly not gates and unchanged by this phase: the bio-suggestion-blocks-every-delete question stays a **backend/product** call outside the frontend wall; selection still does not survive a tab round-trip (fix by passing saved state into `lvTravelDocMount()`, not by keeping a hidden mount alive); narrator `e7fdb578`'s library now holds **five** disposable smoke photos -- the four from Phase 3C plus one this phase's smoke added, because photo *links* cascade with a trip delete and photos do not. Also still open from 2026-07-23: live smoke of `WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01`. A phase is a scope wall.)

- 2026-07-25 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 3D LANDED, smoke-accepted** — route order and route-row ergonomics now exist in the unified Travel Doc workspace, closing the last surface where the legacy Documenter still held a workflow advantage): Front-end only; **no backend, no API, no schema, no flag change**. `server/code/api/routers/trips.py` was read first and needed nothing — all three reorder/move endpoints already exist and validate strictly. **The gap list:** production's board was reviewed against the port and had exactly four affordances the port lacked — region reorder, stop reorder, evidence badges on route rows, and a selectable region row. All four are in and **nothing was retired**; production has no drag-and-drop on either tile, grep-verified rather than assumed, so nothing was copied that does not exist. **The load-bearing decision: a stop move sends two ids, not a permutation.** Production builds the whole sibling list and posts it to `/api/trips/{id}/stops/reorder`, which 400s unless that list is *exactly* the current sibling group — a request precisely as stale as the tree it was built from, so any concurrent insert or delete turns a move the operator can see is legal into a refusal they cannot explain. The port calls `/api/trips/{id}/stops/{stop_id}/move` with `before_stop_id`/`after_stop_id` and lets the backend re-derive the sibling group from two ids that are still true. This is a deliberate divergence and it satisfies Chris's test line *"stop reorder before/after works through existing API shape"* — `/move` **is** the existing shape, and it is what production already uses for cross-region moves. A test bans both `ordered_ids` and `/stops/reorder` inside `moveStopRelative` so the permutation cannot creep back. **A reorder never reparents:** `region_id` and `parent_trip_stop_id` are echoed back unchanged, so a substop moves among its own siblings and only there — that is the one way an up/down arrow could destroy a branch the operator was never shown, and two tests pin it. **Regions still post a permutation because there is no other door:** `/regions/reorder` is the only region-order endpoint, so the staleness is unavoidable there and is handled the way Phase 3B handled the region-delete 409 — the refusal is surfaced in-panel next to the rows it is about and the bundle is reloaded, so the next attempt is built from a fresh tree. It does not dead-end and it never reaches for a native dialog. **Two new `st` fields:** `routeBusy` holds the id of the row whose move is in flight and disables every arrow on the board while set, because two interleaved reorders are each built from the tree as it looked before the other one landed; `routeError` is the in-board failure surface. Both clear in `selectTrip()`, `afterTripDeleted()` and `afterRouteDeleted()`, for the same reason the 3B editors and 3C intake fields do — a move in flight against a row being deleted is a dangling handle of the same kind. **Row selection revived a branch that could never fire:** Phase 3C's `defaultScopeKey()` has a region arm, but `st.routeSel` was written in exactly one place and only ever as a stop, so the upload drawer could default to a region nothing on the surface was able to select. Route rows are now selectable for regions as well as stops, on both the board and the rail. The row body became a `<button>` so selection works from the keyboard as well as the pointer, which is why the first new CSS rule exists: it strips the browser button skin back to the `div` it replaced. All nine new CSS rules are structural and reuse existing `--tdl-*` variables; a test asserts no colour literal on any of them. **Evidence badges cost nothing** — `st.notes`, `st.sources` and `st.photoLinks` already carry `trip_region_id`/`trip_stop_id`, so the badges are a filter over state the bundle already fetched, and a test asserts there is no `api(` call on the badge path. **Verification:** `node --check` clean, `ast.parse` clean, `CR: 0` on all four files on both sides of the transfer, **255 tests green** (was 236) across the same six suites, in the container and on the device. The nineteen new gates in `RouteOrderBoardTest` were **mutation-tested rather than trusted**: nineteen mutants, nineteen killed, each by its intended gate. Seventeen of those gates existed before the live smoke; the last two were written because of it. One mutant exposed a flaw in the harness rather than the code — the anchor `parent_trip_stop_id: parentId,` occurs twice (the 3B stop-editor drawer and the new mover) and the harness correctly refused to mutate an ambiguous anchor instead of silently patching the wrong one; re-run with a two-line unique anchor, it was killed. One test also had to be rewritten after it passed: the CSS check originally sliced from the first Phase 3D class name to end-of-file, which swept in every later block and made the no-new-colour assertion about somebody else's code — it is now line-based over the six Phase 3D class names. **A pre-existing red found and repaired, and it had been red for three phases.** The device's six-suite run came back FAILED (failures=1) while the container's came back OK, and the cause was not this phase: Phase 2 commit `e9b792c` widened the `lv80SwitchPerson` block in `ui/hornelore1.0.html`, but the matching widening of the 1200-character regex window in `tests/test_travel_documenter_panel.py` never got committed, so on the device that window truncated before the `lvShellShowTab("traveldoc")` assertion. **The device's real baseline had been 235/236 since Phase 2, and container-only test runs had been masking it** — an md5 comparison of eight files confirmed this was the only container/device divergence. That repair ships as its **own commit**, separate from Phase 3D, because it is a Phase 2 test-sync fix and not this phase's work. **The live smoke found a defect that nineteen source-scanning gates could not, and that is the most important thing in this entry.** The very first arrow press on the running stack failed: Region WEST's up arrow put `POST /api/trips/{id}/regions/reorder -> 422 [{"type":"model_attributes_type","loc":["body"],"msg":"Input should be a valid dictionary or object to extract fields from"}]` into the board's own error strip, and **every arrow press on the board was broken, regions and stops both**. The cause is a convention the file has and never stated: `api()` owns the request encoding — it stringifies `opts.body` itself, in the branch sitting after the `FormData` check — so a call site must hand it a **raw object**. Both Phase 3D movers were written as `body: JSON.stringify({...})`, which double-encoded the request into a JSON *string*. Two things are worth recording about how this got through. **No source-scanning test could have caught it**, because both spellings look equally plausible read in isolation — the defect lives in the *relationship* between a call site and `api()`, not inside either one, and seventeen gates plus seventeen mutants had just passed straight over it. And **the convention was already unanimous**: every other call site in a 5,500-line file passes a raw object, so the two new movers were the only offenders in the whole file. That is exactly the shape of defect a live smoke exists to catch, and it is the argument for keeping the smoke a gate rather than a formality. The fix is a raw object at both call sites plus two new gates — one bans `body: JSON.stringify` **file-wide** while asserting the encoding still happens exactly once inside `api()` (without that positive half, deleting it from both ends would pass), and one pins that the movers still send a body at all and still leave encoding to `api()` (the file-wide ban alone would be satisfied by a mover that stopped posting anything). Both mutation-tested: reintroducing each `JSON.stringify` gave `FAILED (failures=2)`, restoring gave OK. **The failure surface itself behaved exactly as designed**, which deserves crediting rather than glossing: in-panel, next to the rows it was about, quoting the server verbatim, with a Dismiss control, no native dialog, and a bundle reload that left the board consistent — an operator hitting this could have reported it precisely. **After the fix, all eleven of Chris's live-smoke steps are green**, every tree assertion read server-side from `/api/trips/{id}/tree` rather than off the DOM: disposable trip with three regions and seven stops (a third region was added beyond Chris's two so a middle region exists with both arrows enabled) · region reorder both directions · stop reorder both directions **with the substops travelling with their parent**, so a reorder did not quietly reparent · substop reorder inside its own group · **arrow end-disable is per sibling group, not per screen row** — the last substop's down arrow is disabled even though a top-level stop sits visually below it, and a forced click on that disabled arrow left the tree byte-identical, so the substop could not be lifted out of its group · insert before and insert after on a top-level stop **and on a substop**, the hint reading *"Inserting before N-Alpha-sub (as a child stop)"* with the parent select pre-set, so `insertContext`/`insertHint` are intact and an insert taken against a substop lands in the child sibling group rather than at top level · cross-region move through the stop editor's region select · **unsafe moves blocked at both layers** — the parent select omits the stop's own subtree, and forcing it past the UI with a direct API call returned **400 `move would create a cycle (parent is a descendant of this stop)`** · displayed order surviving a full page reload and remount, twelve board rows in exactly the pre-reload sequence and matching the server tree depth-first · counts refreshing (`3 regions - 9 stops` in the trip header, `7 stops` on the region row, ten day cards each rendering photo/note/source/context counts) · **both Phase 3B delete ladders**, the region one refusing at rung one and escalating to a second rung quoting the server verbatim and naming the blast radius, destroying nothing until the second · **the Phase 3C `FileList` property**, a scope retarget from trip to stop leaving the file input node the *identically same object* still in the document · **the Phase 3A force-delete gate** still arming only on the exact title (empty disabled, `P3D SMOKE` against `P3D SMOKE — disposable` still disabled, exact title armed, force delete giving a server 404 and an empty rail) · **no duplicate mount, socket or listener** across three full legacy round trips (one `.tdl-root`, zero `.td-root`, one host, zero new WebSockets, legacy fallback still reachable) · **zero native dialogs and zero console errors**, the spy empty at every checkpoint. **Files changed:** `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js`, `ui/css/travel-doc-lab.css`, `tests/test_travel_doc_lab.py`, `tests/test_travel_documenter_panel.py`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Files added:** none. **Files removed:** none. **Flag state: unchanged.** **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2 — no extractor-lane code touched. **Open items:** Phase 4 (delete the legacy fallback and the old Documenter), 5 (test consolidation), 6 (full live smoke). Backlog carried, explicitly not gates and unchanged by this phase: the bio-suggestion-blocks-every-delete question stays a **backend/product** call outside the frontend wall; selection still does not survive a tab round-trip (fix by passing saved state into `lvTravelDocMount()`, not by keeping a hidden mount alive); the four disposable Phase 3C smoke photos still sit in narrator `e7fdb578`'s library, stamped `uploaded_by_user_id = "travel_doc_unified"`, and remain a scoped maintenance pass rather than a blocker. Also still open from 2026-07-23: live smoke of `WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01`. A phase is a scope wall.)

- 2026-07-25 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 3C LANDED** — photo upload, source upload and photo clustering now exist in the unified Travel Doc workspace, so the legacy Documenter is no longer needed for intake): Front-end only; **no backend, no API, no schema, no flag change**. `server/code/api/routers/trips.py` was read first and needed nothing — all five endpoints this phase calls already exist. Chris's wall holds: upload/source/cluster only, no backend policy, no route-board redesign, `travel-documenter.js` untouched, legacy fallback still reachable. **This was a capability to build, not a control to port** — the lab contained zero `FormData` and zero file inputs. **The load-bearing constraint is not a backend one, it is `FileList`.** A file input holds a `FileList` that script cannot write, which makes Phase 3B's problem worse rather than similar: a lost text cursor can be restored, a lost file selection cannot. So the upload drawer must not repaint between choosing files and pressing Upload, and it does not — the scope target line swaps its own `textContent`, the file hint counts in place, the title field enables and disables itself, and the submit button toggles its own `disabled`. There is no `renderAll()` anywhere inside either `onchange` handler, and a test pins that absence. **The shape:** three new `st` fields (`uploadDrawer`, `photoIntake`, `sourceIntake`) drive an intake module of roughly four hundred lines — `parseScopeKey`, `scopeChoices`, `defaultScopeKey`, `openUploadDrawer`, `renderUploadDrawer`, `photoUploadPath`, `uploadPhotoFiles`, `uploadSourceFiles`, `runClusterPhotos`, `renderIntakeResult`, `renderPhotoIntakeBar`, `renderSourceIntakeBar`. All three fields clear in `selectTrip()` and in `afterTripDeleted()`, for the same reason the Phase 3B editors do: an intake result describes a trip the operator may no longer be looking at. **`api()` grew a `FormData` branch.** The file has exactly one `fetch(`, and it stringified every body and hand-set `Content-Type: application/json`; a `FormData` must go out untouched and *without* a hand-set content type, because the browser has to write that header itself to append the multipart boundary — and `JSON.stringify(new FormData())` yields `"[object FormData]"`. The branch sits ahead of the JSON branch and a test asserts that **ordering**, not merely that both exist. **Scope is explicit, never ambient — a deliberate divergence from production.** Production's `uploadSourceFiles` reads `editorScope()` at submit time, so retargeting the workspace between choosing a file and pressing Upload silently moves the destination. The port resolves a scope key inside the drawer instead; `defaultScopeKey()` is the only function permitted to read `st.routeSel`, the four upload functions never do, and a test asserts that. The target line spells the destination out in prose, so a wrong scope is visible before the request rather than after. **Intake is not approval.** Nothing here touches the OCR / public-lookup / caption / observation ladder; no `include_in_memoir` and no `trip_day_id` is ever sent on a source upload; day attach stays its own separate act on the Trip Plan tab; evidence lanes stay hide-only and no new DELETE was added. Uploads are stamped `uploaded_by_user_id = "travel_doc_unified"` so intake from this surface is identifiable after the fact — the backend gives special meaning only to `uploaded_from_surface == "travels_shelf"`, which this surface deliberately does not claim. **A same-scope bug found and fixed: three test suites had gone blind.** Phase 3C's code contains `files.accept = "image/*"`. Three suites (`test_travel_doc_evidence_ui`, `test_travel_doc_livetest_fixes`, `test_trip_lane_fixpack_js`) stripped comments with a naive regex that does not know about string literals, so that `/*` opened a phantom block comment swallowing hundreds of lines down to the next real close — three tests failed, one erroring on a substring not found. The repo already had a string-aware stripper, `tests/source_scan_helpers.strip_js_comments`, added under `WO-POST-REVIEW-SAFETY-DRAFT-EXPORT-HARDENING-01` Phase 6.4 for exactly this class of bug but never applied to these three files. They now use it. The assertions were **not** loosened and `image/*` was **not** avoided: the tests were blind, not wrong. That migration lands as its **own commit before** the Phase 3C code, because the Phase 3C code is what makes the naive stripper fail and the other order would leave one red commit on main. **Verification:** `node --check` clean, `ast.parse` clean, CSS braces 344/344, `CR: 0` on all six files on both sides of the transfer. **236 tests green** (was 220) across the same six suites Phase 3B counted, plus a 306-test adjacent sweep green. The sixteen new gates in `TripIntakeUploadClusterTest` were **mutation-tested rather than trusted** — all sixteen passed on the first run, which is suspicious, so the tree was copied aside and four defects introduced (the `FormData` branch disabled, `uploadPhotoFiles` made to re-read the ambient selection, a `renderAll()` added inside `files.onchange`, and `include_in_memoir` appended to the source form); each was caught by exactly its intended gate and by no other. **Live smoke, every one of Chris's twelve steps green:** disposable trip created · region and stop added · photos uploaded at **all three** scopes, not just the one the smoke line minimally required — stop, trip and region, each hitting its own endpoint · Photos tab count climbing 0 -> 2 -> 3 -> 4 with thumbnails rendering · a photo attached to Day 1 through the existing day-attach picker, which offered all four uploads · a source uploaded at stop scope with type `itinerary` and a title · Sources climbing 0 -> 1 · cluster run in-panel · counts refreshed · no auto-promotion · the trip removed through the Phase 3A force-delete gate · mount census clean · **zero native dialogs**, the spy reading empty at every checkpoint. **Three things the smoke proved that a static test could not.** The `FileList` survives retargeting: with two files chosen, changing the scope select from trip to stop left the file input node *identically the same object*, both files still selected, and only the target line's text changed. The panel reports honestly rather than optimistically: a byte-identical duplicate uploaded to a second scope came back *"Ingested: 0."* because the backend deduplicates on hash, and re-running with a genuinely new file gave *"Ingested: 1."* and moved the count — the first result was correct, not a failure, and the panel said so. And no-auto-promotion was verified **server-side, not by reading the UI**: the uploaded source row carries `include_in_memoir = 0`, `trip_day_id = null`, its title and `source_type` preserved, and both `trip_region_id` and `trip_stop_id` set, which is the stop-scope path correctly deriving the parent region. Cluster reported 4 photos considered and 4 links written, all below the confidence threshold, and the counts did not move — correct, because the smoke images are synthetic and carry no EXIF timestamps, and the panel's warning says exactly that. **A probe correction worth recording:** after the force delete the trip rail looked wrongly empty against a probe reporting two trips, and I started to suspect a `loadTrips()` defect. Reading the backend showed `list_trips(person_id: Optional[str] = None)` and that **FastAPI silently ignores unknown query params** — my probe's `narrator_id=` had been returning the *unfiltered* list all along. The rail was correctly empty and the probe was wrong. Any future trip probe must use `person_id=`. **Files changed:** `ui/js/travel-doc-lab.js`, `ui/css/travel-doc-lab.css`, `tests/test_travel_doc_lab.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_evidence_ui.py`, `tests/test_travel_doc_livetest_fixes.py`, `tests/test_trip_lane_fixpack_js.py`, `docs/wo/WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01_Spec.md`, `MASTER_WORK_ORDER_CHECKLIST.md`, `CLAUDE.md`. **Files added:** none. **Files removed:** two `_p3c_*.py` scratch files (container-only; they never reached the repo). **Flag state: unchanged.** **Active baseline unchanged:** `r5h-followup-guard-v1` 78/114, v3=49/72, v2=43/72, mnw=2 — no extractor-lane code touched. **Open items:** Phase 3D (route board / reorder / insert ergonomics), then Phase 4 (delete the legacy fallback and the old Documenter), 5 (test consolidation), 6 (full live smoke). **One honest residue, reported not hidden:** photos belong to the narrator's library, not to a trip, so the four disposable smoke images survived the trip force-delete and are still in narrator `e7fdb578`'s library — identifiable precisely because of the stamp this phase added, since every one carries `uploaded_by_user_id = "travel_doc_unified"`. They were **not** deleted: photo deletion is outside this phase's wall and the doctrine is hide-only. Backlog carried, explicitly not gates and unchanged by this phase: the bio-suggestion-blocks-every-delete question stays a **backend/product** call outside the frontend wall; selection still does not survive a tab round-trip (fix by passing saved state into `lvTravelDocMount()`, not by keeping a hidden mount alive); route board polish and reorder ergonomics; final removal of the legacy Documenter. Also still open from 2026-07-23: live smoke of `WO-EVIDENCE-LIFECYCLE-TRIP-FORCE-01`. A phase is a scope wall.

- 2026-07-25 (**WO-TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY-01 Phase 3B LANDED** — trip create/edit and region/stop CRUD now exist in the unified Travel Doc workspace, so the unified surface can finally do the operator's whole editing job): Front-end only; **no backend, no API, no schema, no flag change**. Chris's order fixed the sequence — empty-state copy, trip create/edit, region CRUD, stop CRUD, insert-at-position semantics, legacy fallback stays reachable — and it holds: no upload, no cluster, no route-board/reorder pass, `travel-documenter.js` untouched, the surface switch still works. **The shape:** six new `st` fields (`tripEditor`, `regionEditor`, `stopEditor`, `routeDelete`, `insertContext`, `tripWarning`) drive four editor drawers and one delete-review ladder; the placeholder `renderCurrent()` is replaced by `renderTripTab()` plus a route board, and the tab it lives on is renamed `current` -> `trip` with a `setTab` back-compat shim so no existing call site breaks. Every drawer holds live DOM references in closures and reads `.value` on submit, so typing never repaints and inputs never lose focus — verified live on the trip title across **19 real keystrokes** and again on the force-delete arming field across **28**. Handlers are assigned by property (`input.oninput = fn`), never `addEventListener`, so reopening a drawer for a different entity cannot stack a stale closure. **The port is deliberately not verbatim in three places, all banner-documented at the head of the file.** The load-bearing one is a **production defect the port refuses to carry**: `DELETE /api/trips/regions/{id}` returns **409** on a region that still has stops unless the caller sends a force flag, but production's `deleteRegion` neither sends it nor handles the 409 — so after the operator answers `window.confirm()`, the request dead-ends and **nothing is deleted, with no message**. The unified port tries unforced first and, on 409, opens a **second review stage** that quotes the backend's own refusal verbatim alongside the lab's independent count from the loaded tree, escalating the button to name the blast radius. Proved live, not theorised: an empty region deleted cleanly at stage 1; a region holding two stops hit the 409, opened stage 2 reading *"Delete region and its 2 stops"*, destroyed nothing at stage 1, and then cascaded correctly when confirmed — the trip's force-delete gate afterwards reported **0 regions, 0 stops**, which is the independent check that the cascade actually landed. **Zero native dialogs, proved by instrumentation rather than inspection.** A spy wrapping the three native dialog functions recorded zero hits across every destructive flow — stop delete, stop delete with children, empty-region delete, both stages of the non-empty-region delete, and two force-delete trip runs. The source is clean by construction too: the delete executor is named `deleteStopReviewed`, not `deleteStopConfirmed`, because the test scanner strips comments but bans the native-dialog call substrings in code. **Insert-at-position semantics are preserved, not approximated.** `insertContext` / `insertHint` carry production's meaning, and the guard `var useCtx = (ctx && regionId === ctx.region_id && parentId === (ctx.parent_stop_id || null)) ? ctx : null;` drops a stale context the moment the operator retargets the drawer to a different region or parent — production had no such check. Live: `+ After` on Munich showed "Inserting after Munich", `+ Before` showed "Inserting before Munich", and the final order came out Frankfurt Airport -> Munich -> Salzburg. Reparenting and cross-region moves work (indent 36px -> 54px, parent dropdown repopulating per region), and **subtree exclusion holds** — editing Munich offered only [Top level, Frankfurt Airport], never its own descendants. **Second finding, fixed in the same pass:** `st.daysWarning` was **read** by the Trip Plan tab but **never set** by anything, a dead banner left over from when the Lab was read-only. `applyTripWarnings()` now sets both it and the new `st.tripWarning`; they render on different tabs, so there is no double display. **Verification:** `node --check` clean, `ast.parse` clean, CSS braces 332/332, `CR: 0` on all four files on both sides of the transfer. **220 tests green** (was 205) across the same six suites Phase 3A counted, plus a 55-test adjacent sweep green. **A note on that baseline:** the container's `CLAUDE.md` is stale and contains no `TRAVEL-DOC-UNIFY` entries at all, so the 205 figure could not be reconciled from it — it was recovered from the device copy. Docs edits belong on the device copy; the container's is a stale cache. **Live smoke, every one of Chris's thirteen steps green:** workspace opens with exactly one `.tdl-root` and zero `.td-root` · the empty-state copy fix is live (*"No trips yet for this narrator.
